# Codex audit: Phase 5.7 V2.5 + V2.6 — ack_handle trait + BlockingTransport fixture (closes Opus V2.4 HIGH-1 V8-block UX hazard)

## Context

V2.5 + V2.6 cycle ships the engine refactor that closes Opus V2.4
HIGH-1 (V8-block UX hazard). Codex-verified PASS-WITH-FINDINGS at
plan time (transcript: `.codex-v2-5-plan-review.out`); all 4
MEDIUM + 1 LOW findings applied to design BEFORE implementation.

State at HEAD:
- engine `1b233af6150` on branch `feat/quantbook-engine`
- IDE `c5997998741` on branch `feat/visualise-v1`

## What V2.5 + V2.6 shipped (engine commit `1b233af6150`)

1. **`Transport::ack_handle(&self) -> Option<Box<dyn FlushAck + Send>>`**
   default-`None` trait method (`crates/ql-collab/src/transport.rs`).
   New `FlushAck: Send` trait with `wait_for_drain() -> Result<(), TransportError>`.
   No `Send` supertrait added to `Transport` (Codex M4 plan fix).

2. **`WebSocketProgressAckHandle`** in `crates/ql-collab-ws/src/lib.rs`
   captures `target: u64` at `ack_handle()` call (Codex M1 plan
   fix: target snapshot AT call site, NOT at wait-start). Mirrors
   `WebSocketTransport::flush_pending`'s wait loop on cloned Arcs.

3. **`BlockingTransport` + `BlockingAckHandle`** (V2.6 test
   fixture) gated behind `test-fixtures` Cargo feature
   (`crates/ql-collab/src/transport.rs`). `block_ms` upper bound
   defensive timeout; `release` + `blocked` Condvar pair.

4. **`CollabSession::flush_pending_handle(&self) -> Option<Box<dyn FlushAck + Send>>`**
   proxy (`crates/ql-collab/src/session.rs`). Takes `&self` — the
   key V2.5 enabler.

5. **`flushPendingToTransport` napi method refactor**
   (`crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs`). V2.5 lock-release
   pattern: extract handle under session lock, drop lock, then
   spawn_blocking-wait without lock held. The CORE V8-block
   closure.

6. **`BlockingTransportFixture` napi class** with constructor +
   `takeTransport()` + `release()` + `waitUntilBlocked()`.
   Mirrors V2.1 LoopbackPair single-use pattern (Codex M2 plan
   fix).

7. Rule 4 compile-asserts: `WebSocketProgressAckHandle: Send`,
   `BlockingTransport: Send`, `BlockingAckHandle: Send`,
   `BlockingTransportFixture: Send`.

8. `crates/ql-collab/src/lib.rs` re-exports `FlushAck` (Codex L1
   plan fix) + `BlockingTransport` / `BlockingAckHandle` behind
   `test-fixtures` feature.

9. IDE pair (`c5997998741`):
   - `extensions/quantlab/src/quantbook/types.ts`:
     `BlockingTransportFixtureInstance` + `Constructor` types +
     module export + rewritten `flushPendingToTransport` JSDoc
     (removed V2.4 V8-block warning, added V2.5 closure note).
   - `extensions/quantlab/src/quantbook/loader.ts`: shape-check
     for `BlockingTransportFixture`.
   - `extensions/quantlab/test/quantbook-roundtrip.test.ts`:
     replaced 2 vacuous V2.4 Loopback tests with 5 new V2.5
     contract tests + V2.6 fixture tests + V2.6 loader-skew test.
     Mocha 77/77 passing locally (was 72).

## What Codex should audit

### Protocol & correctness lane

For each of the 9 ship items above:

1. **Source-walk the actual implementation** at the engine commit
   `1b233af6150`. Don't trust the prose summary — verify the code.
2. **Verify V8-block closure** (THE CORE V2.5 OBJECTIVE): does the
   napi binding actually release the session lock BEFORE the
   Condvar wait? Read the V2.5 `flushPendingToTransport` body at
   `crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs` (around line 832); trace
   the lock guard's lifetime; confirm the spawn_blocking task
   does NOT touch `self.inner`.
3. **Verify Codex M1 contract**: is the target captured at
   `ack_handle()` call, NOT at `wait_for_drain` entry? Read
   `WebSocketTransport::ack_handle` + `WebSocketProgressAckHandle`.
4. **Verify Rule 4 compile-asserts** actually exist + are
   reachable. Grep for the const-fn assert blocks.
5. **Verify the V2.6 fixture's `waitUntilBlocked` signal fires
   BEFORE the Condvar wait enters**: read
   `BlockingTransport::wait_blocked` — does the `blocked` flag
   get set + notified BEFORE the `release` cv.wait?
6. **Verify no FFI-reachable panics introduced**. Grep for new
   `panic!`/`unwrap()`/`expect()` in the V2.5 changes.

### Adversarial / edge cases lane

1. **Race: `detachTransport` mid-wait**. The V2.5 handle holds
   Arc clones of progress fields. JS calls `detachTransport()`
   between handle extraction and wait completion. What happens?
   Per Codex plan review: writer task aborts → closed flag flips
   → wait_for_drain returns Err(Closed) within ~100ms.
   VERIFY this still holds in the V2.5 implementation. Test
   `ack_handle_survives_transport_drop_returns_closed` is in
   `crates/ql-collab-ws/tests/websocket_transport.rs` — does it
   actually exercise this?

2. **CI flakiness of the V2.5 contract test**. The mocha test
   asserts `elapsed < 500` for `opCount()` during a pending
   1000ms-blocked flush. Is the 500ms threshold realistic on
   slow CI? Should we make it more lenient (e.g., `< 800`)?

3. **Concurrent flushPending soundness**. Mocha test
   "concurrent flushPendingToTransport calls serialize through
   ack handle" — does it actually exercise serialization? Two
   spawn_blocking tasks could run on parallel threads of the
   blocking pool; the test only verifies both Promises resolve
   eventually.

4. **`blockMs == 0` semantics**. The BlockingTransport `0` means
   "wait indefinitely until release". The fixture controller
   docstring says this. Is there a test that pins this? Could
   a misuse hang the test runner?

5. **`BlockingTransportFixture` Send claim**. Codex Rule 4
   compile-assert asserts `BlockingTransportFixture: Send`. But
   the fixture has `inner: Option<BlockingTransport>` — is
   `BlockingTransport: Send`? (Yes, per the V2.6 assert.) And
   `Option<Box<dyn Transport + Send>>` from the take-once pattern
   in the binding — is that path sound?

6. **Stale-binary error UX**. The V2.6 loader test fires when
   `BlockingTransportFixture` is missing. Is the error message
   helpful? Does it mention which feature flag the user needs to
   enable? (Recommend the message say "rebuild with
   `--features test-fixtures` on ql-collab".)

7. **Production cdylib carries the fixture**. `ql-bindings-node`'s
   `Cargo.toml` enables `test-fixtures` unconditionally. Per
   Codex plan review Q4 this was the agreed compromise. Re-verify:
   is the fixture genuinely harmless? Can a malicious IDE caller
   construct a `BlockingTransportFixture` + `attachTransport` +
   call `flushPendingToTransport` on a real session and cause DoS?

8. **TS type-safety for `BlockingTransportFixture`**. The IDE
   types declare `BlockingTransportFixtureInstance` + `Constructor`.
   Does this surface require any cast at use site? Could a
   well-meaning IDE consumer accidentally call methods on a
   consumed fixture without runtime error?

9. **Trait stability**. `Transport::ack_handle` was added as a
   default-None method. Verify this doesn't break any existing
   `impl Transport for X` blocks in the workspace (grep all
   `impl Transport for`). Per Codex plan review, the trait stays
   non-supertrait, so this is fine — but verify no `+ Send`
   bound or `where Self: Sized` accidentally crept in.

//! `ql-collab-ws` — WebSocket `Transport` impl for Phase 5
//! collaboration.
//!
//! **Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 4 (2026-05-21, this ship):** first
//! production-grade [`Transport`](ql_collab::Transport) impl. Bridges
//! tokio-tungstenite's async WebSocket client to the sync `Transport`
//! trait via `tokio::sync::mpsc` channels and two spawned background
//! tasks.
//!
//! ## Crate boundary rationale
//!
//! `ql-collab` core is purely sync and embeddable in non-tokio
//! runtimes (e.g., an IDE main thread, future WASM target). Pulling
//! tokio + tokio-tungstenite into the core would force that
//! constraint on every consumer. Keeping the WebSocket impl in a
//! separate crate means:
//! - Consumers who only need `LoopbackTransport` (tests) or a custom
//!   transport (in-process IPC, gRPC streaming) don't pay for tokio.
//! - The async runtime lives at the application boundary, not in the
//!   collaboration layer.
//!
//! ## Architecture
//!
//! ```text
//!  caller (sync, e.g. inside CollabSession::append_op)
//!    │
//!    │  send(bytes)        try_recv() -> Option<bytes>
//!    ▼                            ▲
//!  WebSocketTransport             │
//!    │ outbound_tx              inbound_rx
//!    │  (mpsc::UnboundedSender)  (mpsc::UnboundedReceiver)
//!    ▼                            ▲
//!  writer_task (tokio)          reader_task (tokio)
//!    │  awaits outbound_rx        │  awaits ws_stream.next()
//!    ▼                            ▲
//!    │  ws_sink.send(Binary)      │  inbound_tx.send(bytes)
//!    ▼                            │
//!  ===================== TCP socket =====================
//! ```
//!
//! The two background tasks own the WebSocket halves
//! (`SplitSink` + `SplitStream` via `futures_util::StreamExt::split`).
//! `Drop` aborts both tasks (RAII close).
//!
//! ## V1 limitations (deferred to V2 V4)
//!
//! - **No TLS (`ws://` only).** Use a TLS-terminating reverse proxy
//!   in front, or wait for V2 V4 `rustls` feature.
//! - **No auto-reconnect.** Caller drives via `detach_transport` +
//!   new `connect` + `attach_transport`. The Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 1
//!   `last_flushed_vv = None` reset on attach delivers all accumulated
//!   ops on reconnect (offline-write story).
//! - **Unbounded outbound mpsc queue.** Memory growth is bounded in
//!   practice for the common disconnect case: when the peer closes
//!   the TCP socket, the reader task observes EOF / `Err` and sets
//!   the closed flag; subsequent `send` calls fast-path to
//!   `Err(Closed)` before reaching the unbounded mpsc. The genuine
//!   growth window is the **dead-peer-but-not-closed** scenario
//!   (server stops draining but doesn't tear down the TCP socket):
//!   `ws_sink.send` stalls on TCP backpressure, `outbound_rx` grows
//!   unboundedly, and the reader task may still be receiving heartbeat
//!   frames so `closed` stays false. V2 V4 will switch to bounded
//!   with caller-configurable backpressure policy (clarified in
//!   V2 V3 step 4 audit closure, Opus M5).
//! - **Client-only.** Server-side WebSocket impls use other libraries
//!   (`axum-tungstenite`, `warp::ws`, etc.).
//! - **Drops text/ping/pong/close frames as inbound data.** Loro
//!   payloads are binary blobs; non-binary frames don't carry our
//!   protocol. Close frames trigger reader task exit. Inbound Ping
//!   frames: tokio-tungstenite queues a Pong response on the sink
//!   for the next write — but with no app traffic the Pong is not
//!   flushed until the next app send (clarified in V2 V3 step 4
//!   audit closure, Opus L3). Server-side idle-timeout would then
//!   close the connection. Text frames are silently dropped (a
//!   future protocol extension could add a callback hook).
//!
//! ## Example
//!
//! ```ignore
//! use ql_collab::{CollabSession, PeerId, AutoFlushPolicy};
//! use ql_collab_ws::WebSocketTransport;
//!
//! let rt = tokio::runtime::Runtime::new()?;
//! let ws = rt.block_on(WebSocketTransport::connect("ws://localhost:8080"))?;
//! let mut session = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1))?;
//! session.attach_transport(ws);
//! session.set_auto_flush_policy(AutoFlushPolicy::OnAppend);
//! // ... appends now propagate via WebSocket automatically ...
//! ```

use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, AtomicU64, Ordering};
use std::sync::{Arc, Condvar, Mutex};
use std::time::Duration;

use futures_util::{SinkExt, StreamExt};
use ql_collab::{FlushAck, Transport, TransportError};
use thiserror::Error;
use tokio::sync::mpsc;
use tokio::task::JoinHandle;
use tokio_tungstenite::tungstenite::Message;

/// **V2 V3 step 5 megaudit closure (Opus-B M2, 2026-05-21):**
/// panic-safe task-exit guard. Held by the writer/reader tasks; its
/// `Drop` impl runs even on panic-unwinding. If the task exits
/// without calling [`Self::mark_clean_exit`], the guard treats it as
/// an unexpected exit (panic) and sets `closed = true` + records a
/// `RuntimeError` describing the panic.
///
/// Without this guard, a panic in `ws_sink.send`, `bytes.into()`, or
/// any future inner code would leave the transport in `closed=false`
/// with the task gone — callers would poll `try_recv` forever
/// returning `Ok(None)` and `send` would queue bytes into an
/// orphaned mpsc.
struct TaskExitGuard {
    task_name: &'static str,
    closed: Arc<AtomicBool>,
    last_error: Arc<Mutex<Option<WebSocketError>>>,
    /// **V2 V4 V1 step 1 audit closure (Codex L1 / Opus M2, 2026-05-21):**
    /// notify the `flush_pending` Condvar on panic exit so blocked
    /// callers wake up immediately rather than waiting for the
    /// `wait_timeout` tick. Same Arc as `WebSocketTransport::progress`.
    progress: Arc<(Mutex<u64>, Condvar)>,
    clean: bool,
}

impl TaskExitGuard {
    fn new(
        task_name: &'static str,
        closed: Arc<AtomicBool>,
        last_error: Arc<Mutex<Option<WebSocketError>>>,
        progress: Arc<(Mutex<u64>, Condvar)>,
    ) -> Self {
        Self {
            task_name,
            closed,
            last_error,
            progress,
            clean: false,
        }
    }

    /// Call before the task body exits via any normal path
    /// (Err arm, stream-end, clean recv-None). Skipping this
    /// is the signal that the task panicked.
    fn mark_clean_exit(mut self) {
        self.clean = true;
    }
}

impl Drop for TaskExitGuard {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        if !self.clean {
            // Panic path: stash the panic signal in last_error
            // BEFORE setting closed (so a concurrent reader sees
            // the error first). Per CLAUDE.md no-fallback rule,
            // surface the panic loudly rather than swallowing.
            record_runtime_error(
                &self.last_error,
                format!("ql-collab-ws {} task panicked", self.task_name),
            );
            self.closed.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
            // V2 V4 V1 step 1 audit closure (Opus M2): wake any
            // flush_pending waiter so they observe the closed flag
            // without a 100ms timeout delay.
            self.progress.1.notify_all();
        }
    }
}

/// **V2 V3 step 5 megaudit closure (Opus-B M3, 2026-05-21):** surface
/// poisoned-mutex condition as a `RuntimeError` rather than the
/// previous `.lock().ok()` swallow. A poisoned mutex means a holder
/// panicked while populating the slot — the caller deserves to know
/// the slot's most-recent error was lost.
fn record_runtime_error(slot: &Arc<Mutex<Option<WebSocketError>>>, message: String) {
    match slot.lock() {
        Ok(mut guard) => {
            *guard = Some(WebSocketError::RuntimeError(message));
        }
        Err(poisoned) => {
            // Recover the inner mutex despite poison; overwrite with
            // a message that explicitly notes the poisoning so the
            // caller can detect a prior panic-during-error-stash.
            let mut guard = poisoned.into_inner();
            *guard = Some(WebSocketError::RuntimeError(format!(
                "{message} (note: error slot mutex was poisoned by prior panic)"
            )));
        }
    }
}

/// Errors emitted by [`WebSocketTransport`].
///
/// Distinguishes connect-time errors (`InvalidUrl`, `ConnectFailed`,
/// `HandshakeFailed`) from runtime errors observed by the background
/// reader/writer tasks (`RuntimeError`). Connect-time errors surface
/// as the `Result::Err` from [`WebSocketTransport::connect`]; runtime
/// errors are stashed on the transport and readable via
/// [`WebSocketTransport::last_error`] AFTER the trait `send`/`try_recv`
/// surface `TransportError::Closed`.
///
/// Maps tungstenite errors opaquely — we don't want to leak
/// tungstenite types into the public API.
///
/// **V2 V3 step 4 audit closure (Opus M1, 2026-05-21):** the prior
/// ship discarded background-task errors (`if let Err(_e)`), flattening
/// 8 distinct tungstenite::Error variants into a uniform `Closed` at
/// the trait boundary. This violated the CLAUDE.md no-fallbacks rule
/// (errors must be visible) and made it impossible for IDE consumers
/// driving reconnect handshakes to distinguish "connection lost" from
/// "auth failed". Adds `RuntimeError(String)` variant + `last_error()`
/// accessor; both tasks now populate the shared `last_error` slot on
/// non-graceful exit.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Error)]
#[non_exhaustive]
pub enum WebSocketError {
    /// The URL string failed to parse or is missing required parts.
    /// Per CLAUDE.md no-fallback rule: surface clearly, don't silently
    /// retry with a "fixed up" URL.
    #[error("invalid WebSocket URL: {0}")]
    InvalidUrl(String),

    /// TCP connect failed (refused, timeout, DNS, etc.).
    #[error("WebSocket connection failed: {0}")]
    ConnectFailed(String),

    /// WebSocket protocol handshake failed (HTTP upgrade rejected,
    /// invalid response, etc.).
    #[error("WebSocket handshake failed: {0}")]
    HandshakeFailed(String),

    /// Background reader/writer task observed a runtime failure
    /// (peer reset, IO error, protocol violation, capacity exceeded,
    /// etc.) AFTER a successful connect. Surfaces via
    /// [`WebSocketTransport::last_error`] once the trait-level
    /// `Closed` has been observed.
    #[error("WebSocket runtime error: {0}")]
    RuntimeError(String),
}

/// Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 4 — WebSocket `Transport` impl.
///
/// Bridges async tokio-tungstenite to the sync `Transport` trait via
/// `tokio::sync::mpsc` channels and two spawned background tasks.
/// See module-level docs for architecture diagram.
///
/// ## Construction
///
/// Use [`connect`](Self::connect) — async fn that performs the WS
/// handshake. Caller pattern from sync code:
///
/// ```ignore
/// let rt = tokio::runtime::Runtime::new()?;
/// let ws = rt.block_on(WebSocketTransport::connect("ws://host:port"))?;
/// session.attach_transport(ws);
/// ```
///
/// ## Send + Sync
///
/// `WebSocketTransport: Send + Sync` (Send pinned by the V2 V3
/// step 4 manual `const _ASSERT_WEBSOCKET_TRANSPORT_SEND`, Sync
/// pinned by V2 V4 V1 step 3 `static_assertions::assert_impl_all!` —
/// both compile-time checks at the end of this file). Note: the
/// prior
/// V2 V3 step 4 docstring (and Tier J3 backlog entry) incorrectly
/// claimed `!Sync` based on the intuition that the single-consumer
/// mpsc receiver should prevent sharing. In Rust, `Sync` means "`&T`
/// can be sent across threads" — orthogonal to "`&mut T` access is

/// Errors emitted by `CollabSession` operations.
#[derive(Debug, Error)]
#[non_exhaustive]
pub enum CollabSessionError {
    /// The underlying `OpLog` failed (Loro encode/decode, serde,
    /// etc.). Surfaces the wrapped `OpLogError` for diagnosis.
    #[error("op log error: {0}")]
    OpLog(#[from] OpLogError),

    /// A presence-layer call failed (serde, peer-key parse,
    /// underlying OpLog).
    #[error("presence error: {0}")]
    Presence(#[from] PresenceError),

    /// **Phase 5.4 V1:** a Loro `UndoManager` call (`undo` /
    /// `redo`) returned an error. Reachable when the underlying
    /// document is in an invalid state for the requested
    /// operation (e.g. mid-transaction).
    #[error("undo/redo error: {0}")]
    Undo(#[from] loro::LoroError),

    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 (2026-05-19):** the attached `Transport`
    /// returned an error.
    ///
    /// For `flush_to_transport`: the local `OpLog` is unaffected
    /// (the snapshot export happened first; the failed step was
    /// `Transport::send`). Caller can retry the flush.
    ///
    /// For `poll_remote`: any bytes successfully drained BEFORE
    /// the error were already `merge_bytes`'d into the local
    /// `OpLog` — those merges persist. The error indicates the
    /// transport itself is misbehaving (`Io`) — `Closed` is
    /// handled gracefully by `poll_remote` itself (returns
    /// `Ok(merged)`, see Codex+Opus 5.5 V2 V1 audit closure).
    /// Caller should detach + reattach a new transport instead
    /// of retrying poll.
    #[error("transport error: {0}")]
    Transport(#[from] TransportError),

    /// **Phase 5.3 step 5b (2026-05-20) — production wiring closure
    /// (Opus-A Scenario E HIGH).** `rebuild_workbook` invoked
    /// `replay_into` on the session's `OpLog` and replay failed at
    /// some op index. The fresh workbook constructed internally was
    /// in a HALF-MERGED state when the error fired and was DROPPED
    /// before the error was returned (the wrapper owns it). The
    /// session's `OpLog` is unaffected (replay reads only).
    ///
    /// Carries the underlying [`ReplayError`] via `#[source]` so
    /// callers can `match err.source()` for the original op index +
    /// diagnostic.
    #[error("replay error: {0}")]
    Replay(#[from] ReplayError),
}

/// **Phase 5.3 step 5b (2026-05-20) — production wiring closure
/// (Opus-A Scenario E HIGH):** result of
/// [`CollabSession::rebuild_workbook`]. Combines the replay op count
/// with the two repair reports so callers can log a single
/// diagnostic surface.
///
/// `#[non_exhaustive]` per Codex step 5b audit LOW closure: column
/// repair (Phase 5.3 step 5c) is expected to add another field
/// (`column_repair: ColumnRepairReport`) — external struct literal
/// construction is forbidden so future field additions are
/// non-breaking.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
#[non_exhaustive]
pub struct SyncReport {
    /// Total ops applied by `replay_into`. Same as the success
    /// value of `replay_into` itself.
    pub ops_replayed: usize,
    /// Sheet rename-repair pass diagnostics (formulas rewritten,
    /// ambiguous-skipped rules).
    pub sheet_repair: SheetRepairReport,
    /// Table rename-repair pass diagnostics.
    pub table_repair: TableRepairReport,
    /// Column rename-repair pass diagnostics (Phase 5.3 step 5c —
    /// closes Opus-A megaudit V1 LIM #3 HIGH).
    pub column_repair: ColumnRepairReport,
}

/// Phase 5.3 step 5b audit closure (Opus MEDIUM-2) + step 5c update:
/// one-line `Display` impl for log-line diagnostic use. Format:
/// `"sync: ops=N sheet_rewrites=N(skip=N) table_rewrites=N(skip=N) column_rewrites=N(skip=N)"`.
impl std::fmt::Display for SyncReport {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        write!(
            f,
            "sync: ops={} sheet_rewrites={}(skip={}) table_rewrites={}(skip={}) column_rewrites={}(skip={})",
            self.ops_replayed,
            self.sheet_repair.formulas_rewritten,
            self.sheet_repair.ambiguous_rules_skipped.len(),
            self.table_repair.formulas_rewritten,
            self.table_repair.ambiguous_rules_skipped.len(),
            self.column_repair.formulas_rewritten,
            self.column_repair.ambiguous_rules_skipped.len(),
        )
    }
}

use std::collections::VecDeque;
use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, Ordering};

/// Errors emitted by `Transport` impls.
#[derive(Debug, Error)]
#[non_exhaustive]
pub enum TransportError {
    /// The underlying I/O failed (socket dead, network partition,
    /// etc.). Implementations should distinguish recoverable
    /// (reconnect) from permanent (auth rejected) failures via
    /// variant structure in their own error type, then map to this
    /// variant at the trait boundary if they don't want to expose
    /// implementation details.
    #[error("transport I/O error: {0}")]
    Io(String),

    /// The transport is closed and no further sends are accepted.
    /// Receivers should still drain any already-queued bytes via
    /// `try_recv` before considering the channel done.
    #[error("transport closed")]
    Closed,
}

/// Wire-byte channel for Phase 5 collaboration.
///
/// Implementations push and pull opaque byte blobs. The blobs ARE
/// Loro `LoroDoc::export(...)` snapshots / updates; the
/// transport doesn't interpret them. Framing (where one blob ends
/// and the next starts) is the implementation's responsibility.
pub trait Transport {
    /// Push `bytes` to the channel. Implementations may buffer
    /// internally; the call returns when the bytes are
    /// **queued for send**, not when the remote has received them.
    ///
    /// **V2 V3 step 5 megaudit closure (Codex M1 + Opus-B M1,
    /// 2026-05-21):** for buffered impls (`WebSocketTransport` and
    /// future async transports), there is a window between "send
    /// returned Ok" and "bytes on the wire" where the bytes can be
    /// silently lost (transport dropped, peer disconnect mid-flush,
    /// allocator failure during the writer task). The session's
    /// [`crate::CollabSession::flush_delta_to_transport`] advances
    /// `last_flushed_vv` immediately on `send`'s Ok return, so
    /// `has_pending_flush() == false` alone means "queued to the
    /// currently-attached transport," NOT "the peer has received the
    /// ops." **V2 V4 V1 step 1 (2026-05-21) Tier K1 closure**: call
    /// [`Transport::flush_pending`] after `flush_delta_to_transport`
    /// to block until the writer task has completed `ws_sink.send`
    /// for every queued blob — that provides level-1 (local writer)
    /// ack. For stronger guarantees (peer-application ack, TCP-level
    /// ack), keep the transport attached until you've verified peer
    /// reception out-of-band (e.g., via a custom ack-op layered on
    /// `merge_bytes`). On transport drop or `Err(Closed)`, detach +
    /// reattach a new transport — the V2 V3 step 1 baseline-reset
    /// contract re-sends from empty VV.
    ///
    /// On error, the caller should treat the byte blob as unsent
    /// and re-queue (or surface the error to the user). The
    /// transport will not retry automatically — that policy lives
    /// at the layer above (Phase 5.5 design will pick reconnect
    /// semantics).
    fn send(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(), TransportError>;

    /// Non-blocking receive. Returns `Some(bytes)` if a blob is
    /// queued, `None` otherwise. Implementations choose how often
    /// they poll the underlying channel; the contract is "if you
    /// have something, give it to me, else `None`."
    ///
    /// Returns `Err(TransportError::Closed)` only when the channel
    /// is permanently closed AND its internal queue is empty. A
    /// transient I/O failure during poll surfaces as
    /// `Err(TransportError::Io)`.
    fn try_recv(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, TransportError>;

    /// Read the most recent transport-internal error, if any. Default
    /// returns `None` for impls without a runtime-error concept
    /// (`NoopTransport`, `LoopbackTransport`). Buffered async impls
    /// (`WebSocketTransport`) override to expose the underlying cause
    /// of the most recent task-observed failure.
    ///
    /// **V2 V3 step 5 megaudit closure (Opus-A H1, 2026-05-21):** the
    /// V2 V3 step 4 closure added `WebSocketTransport::last_error()`
    /// on the concrete type for IDE consumers driving reconnect
    /// handshakes — but `CollabSession::attach_transport` moves the
    /// concrete type into `Box<dyn Transport + Send>`, making it
    /// unreachable. Lifting the accessor to the trait + adding
    /// [`crate::CollabSession::transport_last_error`] proxy is the
    /// minimum-viable fix: IDE callers can now distinguish "peer
    /// reset" from "auth rejected" from "capacity exceeded" without
    /// downcasting or holding a parallel handle.
    ///
    /// Returns `Option<String>` (lossy) rather than a structured
    /// error type to keep the trait minimal and avoid leaking
    /// impl-specific types (`WebSocketError`, etc.). Consumers
    /// pattern-match on substring or just display the message.
    ///
    /// Read AFTER observing [`TransportError::Closed`] from `send`
    /// or `try_recv`. Returns `None` for clean shutdowns (caller
    /// `close()`, graceful peer Close frame, transport never failed).
    fn last_error(&self) -> Option<String> {
        None
    }

    /// Block until every byte blob previously queued via `send` has
    /// been written to the underlying transport's wire (e.g. WebSocket

    ///
    /// - Step 1: acquire session lock briefly, call
    ///   `inner.flush_pending_handle()` which proxies to
    ///   `Transport::ack_handle(&self)`. For `WebSocketTransport`,
    ///   this captures `queued_count` as the drain target + clones
    ///   the progress + closed Arcs.
    /// - Step 2: DROP the session lock (`parking_lot::MutexGuard::drop`).
    /// - Step 3: `tokio::task::spawn_blocking(move || handle.wait_for_drain())`.
    ///   The wait runs on a tokio blocking thread, holding ONLY the
    ///   handle's internal Arcs (NOT the session lock).
    ///
    /// Concurrent JS sync method calls on the same session (e.g.,
    /// `opCount`, `appendPutValue`, `pollRemote`) acquire
    /// `self.inner.lock()` immediately while this method's wait
    /// runs — the V8 event loop stays responsive.
    ///
    /// **V2.3+V2.4 soundness retained** (UB + tokio-starvation HIGHs
    /// remain closed):
    /// - `&self`, not `&mut self`. napi-rs codegen produces shared
    ///   `&'static CollabSession`; no aliasing UB possible.
    /// - `spawn_blocking` runs on tokio's blocking pool (default
    ///   512 threads), NOT the worker pool. Condvar wait doesn't
    ///   occupy a worker.
    ///
    /// **Failure modes**:
    /// - No transport attached, OR transport's `ack_handle` returns
    ///   `None` (e.g., Loopback/Noop) → returns `Ok(())` immediately.
    /// - Transport error during the wait → JS Error with the
    ///   `TransportError` `Display` string.
    /// - The `spawn_blocking` task panics → JS Error.
    #[napi(js_name = "flushPendingToTransport")]
    pub async fn flush_pending_to_transport(&self) -> Result<()> {
        // **V2.5 lock-release pattern (Opus V2.4 HIGH-1 closure)**:
        // extract the ack handle while holding the session lock,
        // then RELEASE the lock before awaiting the wait. The
        // scoped lock guard drops on the closing brace per
        // parking_lot's standard MutexGuard semantics
        // (`lock_api-0.4.14/src/mutex.rs::MutexGuard::drop`).
        let handle_opt: Option<Box<dyn ql_collab::FlushAck + Send>> = {
            let inner = self.inner.lock();
            inner.flush_pending_handle()
        };

        // No transport attached (or transport has no ack semantics —
        // Loopback/Noop default impl returns None). The contract
        // matches the V2.4 method: `Ok(())`, NOT a rejection.
        let Some(handle) = handle_opt else {
            return Ok(());
        };

        // Wait on the detached handle. The Condvar wait runs on
        // tokio's blocking pool; the session lock is NOT held.
        tokio::task::spawn_blocking(move || handle.wait_for_drain())
            .await
            .map_err(|e| Error::from_reason(format!("flushPendingToTransport task: {e}")))?
            .map_err(|e| Error::from_reason(format!("{e}")))
    }
}

/// Phase 5.7 V2.2 (2026-05-22) — parse a JS string into
/// [`CoreAutoFlushPolicy`]. JS-idiomatic camelCase + lowercase aliases
/// accepted. Rejects unknown strings with a precise error.
fn parse_auto_flush_policy(s: &str) -> Result<CoreAutoFlushPolicy> {
    match s {
        "disabled" | "Disabled" => Ok(CoreAutoFlushPolicy::Disabled),
        "onAppend" | "on-append" | "OnAppend" => Ok(CoreAutoFlushPolicy::OnAppend),
        other => Err(Error::from_reason(format!(
            "AutoFlushPolicy must be 'disabled' or 'onAppend', got {other:?}"
        ))),
    }
}

/// Phase 5.7 V2.2 (2026-05-22) — render [`CoreAutoFlushPolicy`] as a JS
/// string. Uses canonical camelCase form ("disabled", "onAppend").
///
/// **V2.2 audit closure (Opus HIGH-1, 2026-05-22)**: returns `Result`
/// and throws a JS Error when the engine reports a variant unknown to
/// this binding (forward-compat skew). The prior version returned a
/// `'unknown'` sentinel string — a silent fall-through that violated
/// CLAUDE.md's no-fallback rule. JS code doing `policy === 'onAppend'`
/// would silently miss the new variant and fall through to the
/// `disabled` branch, corrupting reconnect / sync logic. Throwing is
/// the correct fail-loudly path: an engine that ships a new variant
/// ahead of the binding being upgraded surfaces the skew on first
/// `autoFlushPolicy()` call.
///
/// The engine's `AutoFlushPolicy` is `#[non_exhaustive]` so V2.3+ must
/// add new variants here when the engine ships them. The compile-time
/// match-exhaustiveness check helps but doesn't catch new variants
/// (because `#[non_exhaustive]` forces the catch-all arm).
fn auto_flush_policy_to_string(p: CoreAutoFlushPolicy) -> Result<String> {
    match p {
        CoreAutoFlushPolicy::Disabled => Ok("disabled".to_string()),
        CoreAutoFlushPolicy::OnAppend => Ok("onAppend".to_string()),
        // Throw on unknown variant rather than silently returning a
        // sentinel. See V2.2 audit closure note above for rationale.
        other => Err(Error::from_reason(format!(
            "autoFlushPolicy: engine reported unknown variant {other:?} — \
             this binding crate ({}) is older than the engine; upgrade \
             ql-bindings-node to add the new variant's JS string mapping",
            env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),
        ))),
    }
}

   232	    ///
   233	    /// `flush_pending` is blocking-sync (uses `Condvar::wait_timeout`).
   234	    /// Calling it from within a tokio task body will block the
   235	    /// runtime worker. Wrap with `tokio::task::block_in_place` (on
   236	    /// multi-thread runtimes) or `tokio::task::spawn_blocking` (on
   237	    /// any runtime) to avoid stalling other tasks.
   238	    fn flush_pending(&mut self) -> Result<(), TransportError> {
   239	        Ok(())
   240	    }
   241	
   242	    /// **Phase 5.7 V2.5 (2026-05-22) — async-flush ack handle.**
   243	    ///
   244	    /// Return a [`Box<dyn FlushAck + Send>`] that can wait for the
   245	    /// drain target captured AT THIS CALL, from a context that has
   246	    /// already released any outer locks guarding the [`Transport`].
   247	    ///
   248	    /// Default impl returns `None` (transports without async-flush
   249	    /// semantics — Loopback, Noop — keep [`flush_pending`] as the
   250	    /// canonical drain). Production transports with progress state
   251	    /// shared across tasks ([`crate::WebSocketTransport`]'s writer)
   252	    /// override and return a handle cloning their internal
   253	    /// progress `Arc`s.
   254	    ///
   255	    /// # Target-snapshot contract (Codex M1 fix, 2026-05-22)
   256	    ///
   257	    /// Implementors MUST capture the drain target — i.e. the
   258	    /// equivalent of `queued_count.load(...)` for transports with a
   259	    /// writer counter — at this call site, BEFORE returning. The
   260	    /// returned handle's [`FlushAck::wait_for_drain`] then waits
   261	    /// until the writer progress reaches this captured target, NOT
   262	    /// whatever `queued_count` is at wait-start time.
   263	    ///
   264	    /// Rationale: the napi binding pattern is
   265	    ///
   266	    /// 1. acquire session lock
   267	    /// 2. call `transport.ack_handle()` → get handle (target captured here)
   268	    /// 3. release session lock
   269	    /// 4. `spawn_blocking(move || handle.wait_for_drain())`
   270	    ///
   271	    /// Between steps 3 and 4 the JS event loop is free to enqueue
   272	    /// more sends via other session calls. Those sends MUST NOT
   273	    /// extend the wait — the wait is "for sends queued BEFORE this
   274	    /// call", same as [`flush_pending`]'s documented contract.
   275	    ///
   276	    /// # Async-context caveat
   277	    ///
   278	    /// [`FlushAck::wait_for_drain`] is blocking-sync (mirrors
   279	    /// `flush_pending`). The intended caller pattern is to invoke
   280	    /// it inside [`tokio::task::spawn_blocking`] (or equivalent) so
   281	    /// the wait doesn't occupy a tokio worker thread.
   282	    ///
   283	    /// # Why a separate handle (vs `flush_pending(&self)`)
   284	    ///
   285	    /// The `&mut self` on `flush_pending` is enforced because the
   286	    /// engine stores transports as `Box<dyn Transport + Send>` and
   287	    /// proxies via `transport.as_mut()` — the trait object's
   288	    /// dispatch interface requires `&mut self` for any mutator.
   289	    /// Even if `flush_pending` itself doesn't logically mutate
   290	    /// the transport's user-visible state, the call site needs a
   291	    /// `&mut Box<dyn Transport>` and therefore an exclusive
   292	    /// reference to the enclosing session.
   293	    ///
   294	    /// `ack_handle(&self) -> Box<...>` takes only a shared
   295	    /// reference (no mutation; just cloning internal `Arc`s) so the
   296	    /// napi binding can call it while holding only a brief
   297	    /// `Mutex` guard, drop the guard, then run the wait without
   298	    /// any outer lock held. Closes Opus V2.4 HIGH-1 (V8-block UX
   299	    /// hazard) per
   300	    /// `docs/audits/2026-05-22-phase-5-7-v2-4-opus.md:215-248`.
   301	    fn ack_handle(&self) -> Option<Box<dyn FlushAck + Send>> {
   302	        None
   303	    }
   304	}
   305	
   306	/// **Phase 5.7 V2.5 (2026-05-22) — async-flush ack handle.**
   307	///
   308	/// Detached drain-wait abstraction for [`Transport`] impls with
   309	/// progress state shared across tasks (e.g.
   310	/// [`crate::WebSocketTransport`]'s writer counter + Condvar).
   311	///
   312	/// Constructed by [`Transport::ack_handle`]; the implementor
   313	/// captures the drain target at construction time (see
   314	/// [`Transport::ack_handle`] doc for the contract). The handle is
   315	/// `Send` so a caller (typically the napi binding's
   316	/// `flushPendingToTransport`) can move it across a
   317	/// `tokio::task::spawn_blocking` boundary and perform the wait
   318	/// without holding any outer lock guarding the transport.
   319	///
   320	/// **Send + !Sync** is sufficient: the handle is moved into one
   321	/// blocking task and never shared. `Sync` is not required by any
   322	/// V2.5 caller — adding it would defensively allow handle-cloning
   323	/// patterns, but the current single-use pattern is preferable
   324	/// (matches `Box`-not-`Arc` ownership and avoids mistaken
   325	/// concurrent waits on the same captured target).
   326	pub trait FlushAck: Send {
   327	    /// Wait until the drain target captured at handle creation is
   328	    /// reached, OR the underlying transport is closed.
   329	    ///
   330	    /// Mirrors [`Transport::flush_pending`]'s error semantics:
   331	    /// `Err(TransportError::Closed)` if the transport was closed on
   332	    /// entry or mid-wait; `Err(TransportError::Io(...))` for
   333	    /// poisoned-mutex / channel-failure conditions.
   334	    fn wait_for_drain(&self) -> Result<(), TransportError>;
   335	}

   579	/// released, OR until the configured `block_ms` upper bound
   580	/// elapses (defensive — tests never hang if `release()` is
   581	/// forgotten).
   582	///
   583	/// `send` and `try_recv` are no-ops; the fixture tests flush
   584	/// behavior in isolation, not the full send/recv cycle. For
   585	/// 2-peer round-trip tests, use [`LoopbackTransport::pair`].
   586	///
   587	/// Use via the napi-binding-side `BlockingTransportFixture`
   588	/// controller class, which owns the `release` + `blocked` Condvars
   589	/// and exposes `takeTransport()` + `release()` + `waitUntilBlocked()`
   590	/// to JS.
   591	#[cfg(feature = "test-fixtures")]
   592	pub struct BlockingTransport {
   593	    /// Upper-bound wait duration in milliseconds. The wait exits
   594	    /// at the earlier of (a) the `release` flag flipping to `true`
   595	    /// or (b) `block_ms` elapsing. Prevents test hangs.
   596	    block_ms: u64,
   597	    /// Externally-controlled release signal. The
   598	    /// `BlockingTransportFixture` napi class holds the same `Arc`
   599	    /// and flips `*lock = true; cv.notify_all()` on `release()`.
   600	    release: Arc<(Mutex<bool>, Condvar)>,
   601	    /// Externally-observable "wait has been entered" signal. The
   602	    /// `BlockingTransportFixture` napi class's
   603	    /// `waitUntilBlocked()` JS method blocks on this until the
   604	    /// flush task signals it has entered the wait. Makes V2.5
   605	    /// contract tests deterministic (Codex M3 fix, 2026-05-22).
   606	    blocked: Arc<(Mutex<bool>, Condvar)>,
   607	}
   608	
   609	#[cfg(feature = "test-fixtures")]
   610	impl BlockingTransport {
   611	    /// Construct a new `BlockingTransport` with the given upper-
   612	    /// bound block duration. Caller-supplied `release` + `blocked`
   613	    /// Arcs are shared with the binding-side fixture controller.
   614	    ///
   615	    /// `block_ms = 0` means "block indefinitely until released"
   616	    /// (the upper bound is bypassed). Callers should always pass
   617	    /// a defensive non-zero value for hang-protected tests.
   618	    pub fn new(
   619	        block_ms: u64,
   620	        release: Arc<(Mutex<bool>, Condvar)>,
   621	        blocked: Arc<(Mutex<bool>, Condvar)>,
   622	    ) -> Self {
   623	        Self {
   624	            block_ms,
   625	            release,
   626	            blocked,
   627	        }
   628	    }
   629	
   630	    /// Internal wait routine shared by `flush_pending` and
   631	    /// `BlockingAckHandle::wait_for_drain`. Signals the `blocked`
   632	    /// flag + notifies, then enters the wait loop on `release`.
   633	    fn wait_blocked(
   634	        block_ms: u64,
   635	        release: &Arc<(Mutex<bool>, Condvar)>,
   636	        blocked: &Arc<(Mutex<bool>, Condvar)>,
   637	    ) -> Result<(), TransportError> {
   638	        // Step 1: signal "wait entered" so external observers
   639	        // (waitUntilBlocked from JS) can proceed.
   640	        //
   641	        // Use poisoned-mutex recovery via `PoisonError::into_inner`:
   642	        // if a prior wait panicked, the blocked-flag mutex may be
   643	        // poisoned. Recovering is safe because the flag is a
   644	        // monotonic latch (once true, stays true; resetting on the
   645	        // BlockingTransport's next attach is not supported in V2.6
   646	        // — fixtures are single-use, mirroring LoopbackPair).
   647	        {
   648	            let mut flag = blocked.0.lock().map_err(|e| {
   649	                TransportError::Io(format!("BlockingTransport blocked lock poisoned: {e}"))
   650	            })?;
   651	            *flag = true;
   652	            blocked.1.notify_all();
   653	        }
   654	
   655	        // Step 2: wait until release flag flips OR block_ms elapses.
   656	        let (lock, cv) = &**release;
   657	        let mut released = lock.lock().map_err(|e| {
   658	            TransportError::Io(format!("BlockingTransport release lock poisoned: {e}"))
   659	        })?;
   660	        // If block_ms == 0, treat as "wait indefinitely" (no
   661	        // timeout). This is for tests that explicitly want to
   662	        // verify the wait behavior; production usage of this
   663	        // fixture would set a defensive non-zero upper bound.
   664	        if block_ms == 0 {
   665	            while !*released {
   666	                released = cv.wait(released).map_err(|e| {
   667	                    TransportError::Io(format!("BlockingTransport release wait poisoned: {e}"))
   668	                })?;
   669	            }
   670	        } else {
   671	            let deadline = Duration::from_millis(block_ms);
   672	            // Single bounded wait; if not released by deadline we
   673	            // exit normally (defensive timeout — Ok, not Err).
   674	            let (new_released, _timeout) = cv
   675	                .wait_timeout_while(released, deadline, |r| !*r)
   676	                .map_err(|e| {
   677	                    TransportError::Io(format!("BlockingTransport release wait poisoned: {e}"))
   678	                })?;
   679	            released = new_released;
   680	            // (We don't care if it timed out vs released — both
   681	            // are acceptable wait-exit conditions for the fixture.)
   682	            let _ = *released;
   683	        }
   684	
   685	        Ok(())
   686	    }
   687	}
   688

   691	    fn send(&mut self, _bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(), TransportError> {

   693	        Ok(())
   694	    }

   696	    fn try_recv(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, TransportError> {
   697	        Ok(None)
   698	    }
   699	
   700	    fn flush_pending(&mut self) -> Result<(), TransportError> {
   701	        Self::wait_blocked(self.block_ms, &self.release, &self.blocked)
   702	    }

   704	    fn ack_handle(&self) -> Option<Box<dyn FlushAck + Send>> {
   705	        // Codex M1 contract: the ack handle captures all relevant
   706	        // state at THIS call. For BlockingTransport, the "drain
   707	        // target" is conceptual — the handle simply waits on the

   709	        // expectation: the wait completes when release() is called
   710	        // OR block_ms elapses, regardless of when the wait actually
   711	        // starts.
   712	        Some(Box::new(BlockingAckHandle {
   713	            block_ms: self.block_ms,
   714	            release: Arc::clone(&self.release),
   715	            blocked: Arc::clone(&self.blocked),
   716	        }))
   717	    }
   718	}

   725	#[cfg(feature = "test-fixtures")]

   612	        // sole shutdown path under current design is `abort()` on both
   613	        // task handles. The graceful `outbound_rx.recv() -> None ->
   614	        // ws_sink.close()` branch in the writer task is UNREACHABLE

   616	        // fields drop (Rust drop order: body first, then fields in
   617	        // decl order). So `outbound_tx` is still alive when we call
   618	        // `writer_task.abort()`. The graceful arm only fires if a
   619	        // future "soft close" API explicitly detaches `outbound_tx`
   620	        // before drop — not part of the current MVP.

   622	        // `closed = true` ordering: set first so any concurrent
   623	        // sync `send`/`try_recv` observes Closed via the flag check
   624	        // before the task abort propagates.
   625	        //
   626	        // **V2 V4 V1 step 1 (Tier K1):** also notify the
   627	        // flush_pending Condvar — a caller awaiting a long-running
   628	        // flush would otherwise wait until the next wait_timeout
   629	        // tick to observe the closed flag. (Unreachable in practice
   630	        // because Drop is called on a unique `&mut self` so no
   631	        // concurrent flush_pending can be in flight, but kept for
   632	        // safety + future shared-reference APIs.)

   343	impl WebSocketTransport {
   344	    /// Connect to a WebSocket server at `url` (e.g. `ws://host:port/path`).
   345	    ///
   346	    /// Must be called from within a tokio runtime context (the two
   347	    /// background tasks are spawned via `tokio::spawn`). The typical
   348	    /// pattern from sync code is `runtime.block_on(connect(...))`.
   349	    ///
   350	    /// On success, returns a fully-wired transport: send/recv channels
   351	    /// connected, background tasks running. On failure, returns one
   352	    /// of the [`WebSocketError`] variants with the underlying cause.
   353	    pub async fn connect(url: &str) -> Result<Self, WebSocketError> {

   355	        // connect + WS handshake in one call. Distinguish the failure

   641	    fn send(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(), TransportError> {
   642	        // **LOAD-BEARING** (V2 V3 step 4 audit closure, Opus M4): this
   643	        // closed-flag check MUST precede `outbound_tx.send`. The
   644	        // `collab_session_websocket_send_after_close_surfaces_closed`
   645	        // integration test depends on observing `Closed` here (the
   646	        // reader task sets `closed = true` on peer disconnect; the
   647	        // writer task may still be parked on `outbound_rx.recv` and
   648	        // hasn't yet seen the broken sink). If a future refactor
   649	        // reorders this check (e.g., to enable an outbox-queuing-
   650	        // while-closed feature), that test will flake.
   651	        if self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
   652	            return Err(TransportError::Closed);
   653	        }
   654	        // UnboundedSender::send returns Err iff the receiver dropped.
   655	        // The receiver lives in the writer task; if it dropped, the
   656	        // task exited (panic, abort, or graceful). Treat as Closed.
   657	        self.outbound_tx
   658	            .send(bytes.to_vec())
   659	            .map_err(|_| TransportError::Closed)?;
   660	        // **V2 V4 V1 step 1 (2026-05-21) — Tier K1 ack channel.**

   662	        // flush_pending() reads this as the target the writer task
   663	        // must catch up to.
   664	        self.queued_count.fetch_add(1, Ordering::SeqCst);
   665	        Ok(())
   666	    }

   670	        // (transport.rs:112-115): "Closed only when channel

   719	    /// flag transitions.

   722	    /// - `TransportError::Closed` if the transport was already closed
   723	    ///   on entry, OR if the writer task sets closed mid-wait (e.g.,
   724	    ///   ws_sink.send failed).

   726	    ///   panicked writer task. The poisoned-mutex recovery via

   730	    ///   before counter increment); reading + retrying could

   734	        // **V2 V4 V1 step 1 audit closure (Codex M1, 2026-05-21):**
   735	        // honor the documented contract — if the transport is closed

   737	        // whether the local progress counter has already caught up.
   738	        // The prior impl returned Ok in this case, contradicting the

   740	        // both Ok and Err. After this closure, the contract is "Err
   741	        // if closed at any point — entry or mid-wait".
   742	        if self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
   743	            return Err(TransportError::Closed);
   744	        }
   745	        let target = self.queued_count.load(Ordering::SeqCst);
   746	        let (counter_lock, cv) = &*self.progress;
   747	        let mut counter = counter_lock
   748	            .lock()
   749	            .map_err(|e| TransportError::Io(format!("flush_pending lock poisoned: {e}")))?;

   751	            if self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
   752	                return Err(TransportError::Closed);
   753	            }
   754	            let (new_counter, _timeout_result) = cv
   755	                .wait_timeout(counter, Duration::from_millis(100))

   758	        }

  1106	#[test]
  1107	fn ack_handle_wait_for_drain_matches_flush_pending() {
  1108	    // V2.5 contract: WebSocketProgressAckHandle's wait_for_drain

  1115	        let mut ws = WebSocketTransport::connect(&server.url())
  1116	            .await
  1117	            .expect("handshake");

  1119	        for i in 0..3u8 {

  1124	        // THIS call.

  1130	        // for the writer task. The handle holds Arc clones so it
  1131	        // can survive the transport's drop, but here we just verify
  1132	        // wait_for_drain returns Ok with the transport still alive.
  1133	        let start = std::time::Instant::now();
  1134	        let result = handle.wait_for_drain();
  1135	        let elapsed = start.elapsed();
  1136	
  1137	        assert!(result.is_ok(), "wait_for_drain ok, got {result:?}");
  1138	        assert!(
  1139	            elapsed < Duration::from_secs(2),
  1140	            "wait_for_drain blocked unreasonably long: {elapsed:?}"
  1141	        );
  1142	
  1143	        // Drop transport now; handle's Arcs keep underlying state
  1144	        // alive; subsequent wait_for_drain calls (on a SEPARATE
  1145	        // captured handle) would return Closed because writer task
  1146	        // is aborted. We don't re-call wait_for_drain here since
  1147	        // target was captured at ack_handle() time and the writer
  1148	        // already caught up; second call would also return Ok.

   781	    /// Async flush-pending — waits for the attached transport's writer
   782	    /// task to drain (level-1 local ack per V2 V4 V1 Tier K1 contract).
   783	    ///
   784	    /// Returns a JS `Promise<void>` that resolves when:
   785	    /// - the writer has completed `send` for every blob queued AT THIS
   786	    ///   CALL (Codex M1: target captured at handle extraction, NOT at
   787	    ///   wait-start), OR
   788	    /// - the transport has been detached / dropped / closed.
   789	    ///
   790	    /// **V2.5 V8-block CLOSURE** (Opus V2.4 HIGH-1, 2026-05-22):
   791	    ///
   792	    /// - Step 1: acquire session lock briefly, call
   793	    ///   `inner.flush_pending_handle()` which proxies to
   794	    ///   `Transport::ack_handle(&self)`. For `WebSocketTransport`,
   795	    ///   this captures `queued_count` as the drain target + clones
   796	    ///   the progress + closed Arcs.
   797	    /// - Step 2: DROP the session lock (`parking_lot::MutexGuard::drop`).
   798	    /// - Step 3: `tokio::task::spawn_blocking(move || handle.wait_for_drain())`.
   799	    ///   The wait runs on a tokio blocking thread, holding ONLY the
   800	    ///   handle's internal Arcs (NOT the session lock).
   801	    ///
   802	    /// Concurrent JS sync method calls on the same session (e.g.,
   803	    /// `opCount`, `appendPutValue`, `pollRemote`) acquire
   804	    /// `self.inner.lock()` immediately while this method's wait
   805	    /// runs — the V8 event loop stays responsive.
   806	    ///
   807	    /// **V2.3+V2.4 soundness retained** (UB + tokio-starvation HIGHs
   808	    /// remain closed):
   809	    /// - `&self`, not `&mut self`. napi-rs codegen produces shared
   810	    ///   `&'static CollabSession`; no aliasing UB possible.
   811	    /// - `spawn_blocking` runs on tokio's blocking pool (default
   812	    ///   512 threads), NOT the worker pool. Condvar wait doesn't
   813	    ///   occupy a worker.
   814	    ///
   815	    /// **Failure modes**:
   816	    /// - No transport attached, OR transport's `ack_handle` returns
   817	    ///   `None` (e.g., Loopback/Noop) → returns `Ok(())` immediately.
   818	    /// - Transport error during the wait → JS Error with the
   819	    ///   `TransportError` `Display` string.
   820	    /// - The `spawn_blocking` task panics → JS Error.
   821	    #[napi(js_name = "flushPendingToTransport")]
   822	    pub async fn flush_pending_to_transport(&self) -> Result<()> {
   823	        // **V2.5 lock-release pattern (Opus V2.4 HIGH-1 closure)**:
   824	        // extract the ack handle while holding the session lock,
   825	        // then RELEASE the lock before awaiting the wait. The
   826	        // scoped lock guard drops on the closing brace per
   827	        // parking_lot's standard MutexGuard semantics
   828	        // (`lock_api-0.4.14/src/mutex.rs::MutexGuard::drop`).
   829	        let handle_opt: Option<Box<dyn ql_collab::FlushAck + Send>> = {
   830	            let inner = self.inner.lock();
   831	            inner.flush_pending_handle()
   832	        };
   833	
   834	        // No transport attached (or transport has no ack semantics —
   835	        // Loopback/Noop default impl returns None). The contract
   836	        // matches the V2.4 method: `Ok(())`, NOT a rejection.
   837	        let Some(handle) = handle_opt else {
   838	            return Ok(());
   839	        };
   840	
   841	        // Wait on the detached handle. The Condvar wait runs on
   842	        // tokio's blocking pool; the session lock is NOT held.

  1015	/// **Why this intermediate class** (Phase 5.7 V2.1 design note,
  1016	/// 2026-05-22): napi-rs's `#[napi(factory, ...)]` can only return a
  1017	/// single `Self`-typed class instance. Returning a 2-tuple or
  1018	/// `Vec<Transport>` fails the trait bound `ObjectFinalize` napi-rs
  1019	/// imposes on factory returns. The cleanest pattern that keeps each
  1020	/// `Transport` end as a separately-attachable class is to expose the
  1021	/// pair-creation step as its own short-lived class with two takers.
  1022	///
  1023	/// **Single-use takers**: each of `takeA` / `takeB` can be called once
  1024	/// per `LoopbackPair` instance. Calling a second time on the same end
  1025	/// returns a JS Error. (Calling a non-yet-taken end after the other has
  1026	/// been taken still works — the two ends are independent.)
  1027	#[napi]
  1028	pub struct LoopbackPair {
  1029	    // Both ends live here until taken. The pair is constructed eagerly
  1030	    // in `new()` so the shared queue exists before either end is
  1031	    // distributed.
  1032	    a: Option<LoopbackTransport>,
  1033	    b: Option<LoopbackTransport>,
  1034	}
  1035	
  1036	impl Default for LoopbackPair {
  1037	    fn default() -> Self {
  1038	        Self::new()
  1039	    }
  1040	}

  1042	#[napi]
  1043	impl LoopbackPair {
  1044	    /// Construct a fresh Loopback pair. Both ends start un-taken.
  1045	    #[napi(constructor)]
  1046	    pub fn new() -> Self {
  1047	        let (a, b) = LoopbackTransport::pair();
  1048	        Self {
  1049	            a: Some(a),
  1050	            b: Some(b),
  1051	        }
  1052	    }
  1053	
  1054	    /// Take ownership of end A. Errors if already taken.
  1055	    #[napi(js_name = "takeA")]
  1056	    pub fn take_a(&mut self) -> Result<Transport> {
  1057	        let end = self.a.take().ok_or_else(|| {
  1058	            Error::from_reason("LoopbackPair.takeA already called on this pair".to_string())
  1059	        })?;
  1060	        Ok(Transport {
  1061	            inner: Some(Box::new(end)),
  1062	        })
  1063	    }
  1064	
  1065	    /// Take ownership of end B. Errors if already taken.
  1066	    #[napi(js_name = "takeB")]
  1067	    pub fn take_b(&mut self) -> Result<Transport> {
  1068	        let end = self.b.take().ok_or_else(|| {
  1069	            Error::from_reason("LoopbackPair.takeB already called on this pair".to_string())
  1070	        })?;
  1071	        Ok(Transport {
  1072	            inner: Some(Box::new(end)),

   760	    /// Opus-B LOW-3 (asymmetry between attach + detach).
   761	    #[must_use = "drop the returned prior transport to release its background tasks; \
   762	                  holding it past attach keeps the old TCP socket alive"]
   763	    pub fn attach_transport_boxed(
   764	        &mut self,
   765	        transport: Box<dyn Transport + Send>,
   766	    ) -> Option<Box<dyn Transport + Send>> {
   767	        // **V2 V3 step 1 contract**: every attach resets the VV baseline
   768	        // so the next flush sends from empty. A new transport-peer hasn't
   769	        // seen ANY of this session's ops; without this reset they'd miss
   770	        // ops 0..stale_vv and end up with a corrupt view. This is the
   771	        // authoritative site for the invariant — `attach_transport<T>`
   772	        // delegates here (V2.1 refactor).
   773	        self.last_flushed_vv = None;
   774	        self.transport.replace(transport)
   775	    }
   776	
   777	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19):** detach the current
   778	    /// transport. Returns it for caller cleanup; returns `None`
   779	    /// if no transport was attached.
   780	    ///
   781	    /// **V2 V3 step 5 megaudit closure (Opus-A M3, 2026-05-21):**
   782	    /// the returned `Box` owns the transport's background tasks
   783	    /// (e.g., `WebSocketTransport`'s reader + writer). Drop the box
   784	    /// to release them — holding it past the reconnect handshake
   785	    /// keeps the old TCP socket alive + the old `last_error()`
   786	    /// reachable. The typical pattern is `let _ = session.detach_transport();`
   787	    /// before attaching a new one.
   788	    #[must_use = "drop the returned transport to release its background tasks; \
   789	                  holding it past detach keeps the old TCP socket alive"]
   790	    pub fn detach_transport(&mut self) -> Option<Box<dyn Transport + Send>> {
   791	        // Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 1: orphaned VV is meaningless. Clear so
   792	        // a subsequent `attach_transport` lands on a clean baseline.
   793	        self.last_flushed_vv = None;
   794	        self.transport.take()
   795	    }

   815	    ///

   817	    /// dyn-compatible without leaking impl-specific error types

   823	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V4 V1 step 1 (2026-05-21) — Tier K1 ack channel.**
   824	    /// Block until every byte blob previously queued via the attached
   825	    /// transport's `send` has reached the wire. Returns `Ok(())`
   826	    /// immediately if no transport is attached.
   827	    ///
   828	    /// Closes the V2 V3 step 5 megaudit's convergent finding (Codex
   829	    /// M1 + Opus-B M1): after `flush_delta_to_transport()` returns
   830	    /// `Ok(true)`, the bytes are queued in the (buffered)
   831	    /// transport's internal channel, NOT yet on the wire. Without
   832	    /// this proxy, `has_pending_flush() == false` could be observed
   833	    /// while bytes have not actually been written. Calling
   834	    /// `flush_pending_to_transport()` AFTER `flush_delta_to_transport`
   835	    /// (or any mutator under `OnAppend`) closes that gap — Ok return
   836	    /// confirms the local writer task has caught up, providing a
   837	    /// level-1 ack (bytes hit the underlying transport's wire).
   838	    ///
   839	    /// # Errors
   840	    /// - `Err(CollabSessionError::Transport(Closed))` if the transport
   841	    ///   closed before completing the drain (e.g., writer task failed
   842	    ///   mid-flush; reconnect via `detach_transport` + new
   843	    ///   `attach_transport` will redeliver the bytes via the V2 V3

   904	/// `CollabSession::attach_transport<T>` accepts any `T: Transport + Send +

   906	///   1. Added `CollabSession::attach_transport_boxed(Box<dyn Transport + Send>)`
   907	///      on the Rust side as a sibling entry point (V2.1 cycle).
   908	///   2. Expose this opaque `Transport` class wrapping the boxed trait
   909	///      object. JS code obtains instances from static factories
   910	///      (`Transport.loopbackPair()` here; `Transport.websocketConnect(url)`
   911	///      in V2.3) and passes them to `CollabSession.attachTransport(t)`.
   912	///
   913	/// **Single-use semantics**: an instance owns its boxed trait object.
   914	/// `CollabSession.attachTransport` MOVES the box out of this wrapper,
   915	/// leaving it consumed. Subsequent attach calls with the same wrapper
   916	/// return a JS Error ("Transport has already been consumed by attachTransport").

   124		});

   126		test('round-trip: peer A append -> export -> peer B merge -> peer B op count matches', () => {
   127			const peerA = createSession(1n);
   128			peerA.appendPutValue(0, 0, 0, 42);
   129			peerA.appendPutValue(0, 0, 1, 100);
   130			peerA.appendPutValue(0, 1, 0, -7.5);
   131			const aOps = peerA.opCount();
   132			assert.strictEqual(aOps, 3);
   133	
   134			const bytes = peerA.exportBytes();
   135			assert.ok(bytes instanceof Uint8Array, 'export returns Uint8Array');
   136			assert.ok(bytes.length > 0, 'export bytes non-empty');
   137	
   138			const peerB = createSession(2n);
   139			assert.strictEqual(peerB.opCount(), 0);
   140	
   141			const merged = peerB.mergeBytes(bytes);
   142			assert.ok(merged >= 1, `merged at least 1 op, got ${merged}`);
   143			assert.strictEqual(peerB.opCount(), aOps,
   144				`peer B opCount should match peer A's (${aOps}), got ${peerB.opCount()}`);
   145		});
   146	
   147		test('fromSnapshot reconstructs the same state as merge', () => {
   148			const peerA = createSession(1n);

   152	
   153			const peerC = sessionFromSnapshot(3n, bytes);
   154			assert.strictEqual(peerC.peerId(), 3n, 'fromSnapshot uses provided peerId, not snapshot origin');
   155			assert.strictEqual(peerC.opCount(), peerA.opCount(),
   156				'fromSnapshot reconstructs the imported op count');
   157		});

   159		test('mergeBytes of empty array preserves op_count without panic', () => {
   160			// **V1 audit closure (Opus M6, 2026-05-22):** loosen the
   161			// assertion to match the engine-side pinned contract at
   162			// `ql-oplog/src/log.rs` `merge_bytes_with_empty_slice_does_not_panic`.
   163			// The engine pins "no panic + no partial state" -- it
   164			// accepts either Ok-with-unchanged-len OR Err-with-unchanged-len.
   165			// Loro 1.12 currently returns Err, but a future Loro version
   166			// may accept empty as a no-op. Either is valid; the strict
   167			// "must throw" assertion would silently break on Loro upgrade.
   168			const s = createSession(1n);
   169			const preCount = s.opCount();
   170			try {
   171				s.mergeBytes(new Uint8Array(0));
   172			} catch {
   173				// Err arm is acceptable.
   174			}
   175			assert.strictEqual(s.opCount(), preCount,
   176				'op_count unchanged after empty merge regardless of Ok/Err outcome');
   177		});

   191		test('cache poisoning regression -- partial exports fail on every call', () => {
   192			// **V1 audit closure (Opus H1 + Codex M1):** if dlopen returns
   193			// a module missing expected exports, the loader must throw
   194			// on EVERY call, not just the first. The original implementation
   195			// set `cachedModule` BEFORE validation, so the second call
   196			// short-circuited via the cache and returned the bad module.
   197			// Closure: cache only AFTER validation passes.
   198			const originalDlopen = process.dlopen;
   199			_resetQuantbookEngineCacheForTests();
   200			// Inject a partial module via monkey-patched dlopen.
   201			(process as unknown as { dlopen: typeof process.dlopen }).dlopen =
   202				(mod: NodeJS.Module): void => {
   203					(mod as unknown as { exports: Record<string, unknown> }).exports = { foo: 1 };
   204				};
   205			try {
   206				assert.throws(
   207					() => loadQuantbookEngine(),
   208					/missing expected exports/,
   209					'first call must throw on partial exports',
   210				);
   211				assert.throws(
   212					() => loadQuantbookEngine(),
   213					/missing expected exports/,
   214					'second call must also throw (cache must NOT poison)',
   215				);
   216			} finally {
   217				process.dlopen = originalDlopen;
   218				_resetQuantbookEngineCacheForTests();
   219			}
   220		});

   233		// Phase 5.7 V2.5 (2026-05-22) -- async Transport surface, V8-BLOCK CLOSED
   234		// =====================================================================
   235	
   236		/**
   237		 * Async flush-pending. Waits for the attached transport's writer
   238		 * task to drain blobs queued AT THIS CALL (Codex M1 contract:
   239		 * target captured at handle extraction, NOT at wait-start).
   240		 *
   241		 * **V2.5 V8-block closure (2026-05-22)**: V2.4 reintroduced this
   242		 * soundly (closed V2.3 UB + tokio-starvation HIGHs via the
   243		 * `Arc<parking_lot::Mutex<...>>` refactor) BUT held the session
   244		 * lock during the Condvar wait -- Opus V2.4 HIGH-1 flagged the
   245		 * resulting V8-block UX hazard. V2.5 closes it: the binding now
   246		 * extracts a detached `FlushAck` handle while holding the session
   247		 * lock, drops the lock, then performs the Condvar wait without
   248		 * the session lock held. Concurrent JS sync method calls on the
   249		 * same session acquire the lock immediately -- the V8 event loop
   250		 * stays responsive.
   251		 *
   252		 * **V2.3 + V2.4 soundness retained**:
   253		 * - `&self`, not `&mut self`. napi-rs codegen produces shared `&`;
   254		 *   no aliasing UB possible.
   255		 * - `spawn_blocking` runs the wait on tokio's blocking pool, NOT
   256		 *   the worker pool. No runtime starvation.
   257		 *
   258		 * Resolves when:
   259		 * - writer has completed `send` for every blob queued at this call, OR
   260		 * - transport has been detached / dropped / closed.
   261		 *
   262		 * **No-op on no transport, or transports with no async-flush
   263		 * semantics (Loopback, Noop)**: returns resolved Promise (NOT a
   264		 * rejection).
   265		 *
   266		 * @throws Error if the transport reports an error, or if the
   267		 *               `spawn_blocking` task panics.
   268		 */
   269		flushPendingToTransport(): Promise<void>;
   270	}

   276	/**

   285	 * Mirrors V2.1 `LoopbackPair`'s single-use take pattern: construct
   286	 * the fixture, call `takeTransport()` once to obtain a `Transport`
   287	 * for `attachTransport(t)`, then drive `release()` + `waitUntilBlocked()`
   288	 * from test code.
   289	 */
   290	export interface BlockingTransportFixtureInstance {
   291		/**
   292		 * Take ownership of the inner `BlockingTransport`, wrapped in a
   293		 * `Transport` instance attachable to a `CollabSession`.
   294		 *
   295		 * Single-use: errors on the second call. The fixture controller
   296		 * retains its `release` + `blocked` Condvars after the take so
   297		 * `release()` + `waitUntilBlocked()` can drive the transport
   298		 * that now lives inside a `CollabSession`.
   299		 *
   300		 * @throws Error on the second call.
   301		 */

   304		/**
   305		 * Flip the release Condvar so any in-progress `flush_pending`
   306		 * or `wait_for_drain` exits. Idempotent (calling twice is safe).
   307		 */
   308		release(): void;

   310		/**
   311		 * Async wait until the fixture's wait routine has actually

   315		 * **Codex M3 fix (2026-05-22)**: deterministic synchronization
   316		 * point for V2.5 contract tests. Without it, an `opCount()` call
   317		 * during a pending `flushPendingToTransport` could race ahead of
   318		 * the `spawn_blocking` task and pass vacuously.
   319		 */
   320		waitUntilBlocked(): Promise<void>;

   331		 * @throws Error if `blockMs` is non-finite / negative / fractional / out-of-u32.

   333		new(blockMs: number): BlockingTransportFixtureInstance;

  1227			//   2. Attach the fixture's transport to a session.
  1228			//   3. Start flushPendingToTransport (the spawn_blocking task
  1229			//      starts; engine-side wait enters Condvar).

  1231			//      synchronization point (Codex M3 fix): we are NOW sure

  1233			//      blocked in wait_for_drain.

  1237			//      lock was held during the wait.

     6	/**
     7	 * Phase 5.7 V1 (2026-05-22) -- thin TypeScript wrapper over the napi

     9	 *
    10	 * The wrapper exists so:
    11	 * 1. Consumer code imports a STABLE TS-side identifier
    12	 *    (`QuantbookSession`) instead of reaching into a dynamically-loaded
    13	 *    module. The loader is hot-pathed once at startup; the wrapper
    14	 *    holds the resolved native class.
    15	 * 2. We can attach JSDoc + IDE-side validation that the napi layer
    16	 *    doesn't (e.g., reject negative `sheet` integers before the BigInt
    17	 *    boundary makes the error less actionable).
    18	 * 3. V2 / V3 can grow the wrapper without changing the napi surface
    19	 *    (e.g., add async retry wrappers around `mergeBytes`).
    20	 *
    21	 * **Per V1 scope:** no Transport, no presence, no undo, no formula
    22	 * support. See engine repo's `docs/phase5/5-7-v1-exit-packet.md`
    23	 * section "V1 deferred to V2" for the full V1 deferred list
    24	 * (engine + IDE).
    25	 */
    26	
    27	import { loadQuantbookEngine } from './loader';
    28	import type {
    29		AutoFlushPolicy,
    30		CollabSessionInstance,
    31		LoopbackPairInstance,
    32		TransportInstance,
    33	} from './types';
    34	
    35	/**
    36	 * Construct a fresh `CollabSession` for the given peer.
    37	 *
    38	 * @param peerId Non-zero u64-domain peer identifier. Throws if `0n`,
    39	 *               negative, or doesn't fit in u64.
    40	 */
    41	export function createSession(peerId: bigint): CollabSessionInstance {
    42		const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
    43		return new engine.CollabSession(peerId);
    44	}
    45	
    46	/**
    47	 * Append a `PutValue` op with JS-side input validation that the napi
    48	 * layer cannot perform (due to ECMAScript ToUint32 coercion happening
    49	 * BEFORE the Rust code sees the value).
    50	 *
    51	 * **V1 audit closure (Opus H2 + Codex H3, 2026-05-22)**: napi-rs's
    52	 * `napi_get_value_uint32` applies ECMAScript ToUint32 to JS Number
    53	 * arguments. That converts:
    54	 *   - `-1` to `0xFFFFFFFF` (silent wrap)
    55	 *   - `NaN` to `0` (silent coercion)
    56	 *   - `Infinity` to `0` (silent coercion)
    57	 *   - `2.5` to `2` (silent floor-toward-zero)
    58	 *
    59	 * The Rust binding sees the post-ToUint32 value and has no way to
    60	 * detect the original JS-side intent. Validate at the TS layer
    61	 * BEFORE the call so each failure surfaces a precise error.
    62	 *
    63	 * @throws Error if sheet/row/col aren't non-negative integers in range,
    64	 *               or if value isn't a finite number.
    65	 */
    66	export function appendPutValueValidated(
    67		session: CollabSessionInstance,
    68		sheet: number,
    69		row: number,
    70		col: number,
    71		value: number,
    72	): void {
    73		if (!Number.isInteger(sheet) || sheet < 0 || sheet > 0xFFFF) {
    74			throw new Error(`appendPutValue: sheet must be an integer in [0, 65535], got ${sheet}`);
    75		}
    76		if (!Number.isInteger(row) || row < 0 || row > 0xFFFFFFFF) {
    77			throw new Error(`appendPutValue: row must be an integer in [0, 4294967295], got ${row}`);
    78		}
    79		if (!Number.isInteger(col) || col < 0 || col > 0xFFFFFFFF) {
    80			throw new Error(`appendPutValue: col must be an integer in [0, 4294967295], got ${col}`);
    81		}
    82		if (typeof value !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(value)) {
    83			throw new Error(`appendPutValue: value must be a finite number, got ${value}`);
    84		}
    85		session.appendPutValue(sheet, row, col, value);
    86	}
    87	
    88	/**
    89	 * Reconstruct a `CollabSession` from a previously-exported snapshot.
    90	 *
    91	 * @param peerId Non-zero u64-domain peer identifier for THIS session
    92	 *               (need not match the snapshot's origin peer; CRDT will
    93	 *               author new ops under this id).
    94	 * @param bytes  Snapshot bytes from another session's `exportBytes()`.
    95	 */
    96	export function sessionFromSnapshot(peerId: bigint, bytes: Uint8Array): CollabSessionInstance {
    97		const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
    98		return engine.CollabSession.fromSnapshot(peerId, bytes);
    99	}

   113	/**
   114	 * Construct a fresh `LoopbackPair` and immediately take both ends.
   115	 * Returns the two Transport instances ready to attach to a session
   116	 * each.
   117	 *
   118	 * Combines `new LoopbackPair()` + `takeA()` + `takeB()` into a single
   119	 * call because the typical use case wants both ends. For cases where
   120	 * one end is created independently (e.g., delayed peer connection),
   121	 * use `createLoopbackPair()` instead and call `takeA` / `takeB`
   122	 * separately.
   123	 *
   124	 * @returns `[transportA, transportB]` where bytes sent on A arrive
   125	 *           at B's `pollRemote` and vice versa.
   126	 *
   127	 * @example
   128	 * ```ts
   129	 * const [tA, tB] = loopbackTransportPair();
   130	 * sessionA.attachTransport(tA);
   131	 * sessionB.attachTransport(tB);
   132	 * // mutate sessionA, flushToTransport, pollRemote on sessionB
   133	 * ```
   134	 */
   135	export function loopbackTransportPair(): [TransportInstance, TransportInstance] {
   136		const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
   137		const pair = new engine.LoopbackPair();
   138		return [pair.takeA(), pair.takeB()];
   139	}
   140	
   141	/**
   142	 * Construct an empty `LoopbackPair` without taking either end. Use
   143	 * when the two takes need to happen at different times.

   145	export function createLoopbackPair(): LoopbackPairInstance {

   161	export function isAutoFlushPolicy(value: unknown): value is AutoFlushPolicy {

   362	// =====================================================================
   363	// Phase 5.7 V2.1 (2026-05-22) -- Transport binding type declarations
   364	// =====================================================================
   365	
   366	/**
   367	 * Opaque wrapper for a `Box<dyn ql_collab::Transport + Send>`.
   368	 *
   369	 * **Single-use semantics**: an instance owns its boxed trait object.
   370	 * `CollabSession.attachTransport(t)` MOVES the box out, leaving the
   371	 * wrapper consumed. After consumption, `isAttachable()` returns
   372	 * `false` and subsequent `attachTransport` calls with the same
   373	 * wrapper throw.
   374	 *
   375	 * Instances are obtained from factory classes (V2.1: `LoopbackPair`;
   376	 * V2.3+: `Transport.websocketConnect(url)`).
   377	 */
   378	export interface TransportInstance {
   379		/**
   380		 * `true` while this wrapper still owns its inner transport.
   381		 * `false` after passing to `attachTransport` (or any other
   382		 * future API that consumes the wrapper).
   383		 *
   384		 * Useful for branching without exception handling:
   385		 * ```ts
   386		 * if (transport.isAttachable()) {
   387		 *   session.attachTransport(transport);
   388		 * }
   389		 * ```
   390		 */
   391		isAttachable(): boolean;
   392	}
   393	
   394	/**
   395	 * Two-ended in-process Transport pair (`LoopbackTransport`).
   396	 *
   397	 * **V2.1 entry point** for obtaining paired Transport instances. The
   398	 * pair's two ends share an in-process queue: bytes sent on end A
   399	 * arrive at end B's `try_recv` and vice versa.
   400	 *
   401	 * Each end can be `take`-n once. Calling `takeA` (or `takeB`) a
   402	 * second time on the same pair throws. The two takes are
   403	 * independent (taking A doesn't affect taking B).
   404	 */
   405	export interface LoopbackPairInstance {
   406		/**
   407		 * Take ownership of end A. Each `LoopbackPair` instance can have
   408		 * `takeA` called once.
   409		 * @throws Error on the second call.
   410		 */
   411		takeA(): TransportInstance;
   412	
   413		/**
   414		 * Take ownership of end B. Each `LoopbackPair` instance can have
   415		 * `takeB` called once.
   416		 * @throws Error on the second call.
   417		 */
   418		takeB(): TransportInstance;
   419	}
