docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:545:**Status (2026-05-22): Phase 5 V1 + D-1 + 5.3 + 5.5 V2 V2 + 5.5 V2 V3 V1 (steps 1-6) + 5.5 V2 V4 V1 (12/13 Tier items; K4 chunking deferred to V2 V4 V2) + 5.7 V1 + 5.7 V2.1 + 5.7 V2.2 + 5.7 V2.3 + 5.7 V2.4 COMPLETE.** Canonical records: `docs/phase5/v1-exit-packet.md` (V1) + `docs/phase5/d-1-exit-packet.md` (D-1 closure) + `docs/phase5/5-3-exit-packet.md` (5.3 closure) + `docs/phase5/v2-v3-exit-packet.md` (V2 V3 V1 closure) + `docs/phase5/v2-v4-v1-exit-packet.md` (V2 V4 V1 closure) + `docs/phase5/5-7-v1-exit-packet.md` (5.7 V1 closure). V2 exit packet pending (lands at 5.7 V2.8 megaudit). Remaining: 5.7 V2.5 engine refactor for V8-block hazard (~1d), 5.7 V2.6 test-only blocking transport fixture (~half-day), 5.7 V2.7 multi-window IDE demo (~1-2d), 5.7 V2.8 3-way megaudit + V2 exit packet (~2-3d). Then 5.7 V3 cell-grid UI + persistence (~1-2wk), 5.8 Phase 5 megaudit (separate, ~4-6d, depends on 5.7 V2+ complete), V2 V4 V2 K4 chunking (~2-3d). **V2.1+V2.2+V2.3+V2.4 audit ladder: 8 audit cycles (4 ship + 4 closure), 8 HIGH + 27 MEDIUM closed cumulatively, Rule 4 paid off 5× across V1+V2.3+V2.4.**
docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:604:   **V2.3 (cycle 3)** — async surface (`Transport.websocketConnect`, `flushPendingToTransport`). Engine `1e2354cb7b1` → `ea07bc6af4e`. IDE `6616e28a2a3` → `233957ab140`. **Audit FAIL** on first ship — Codex+Opus convergent on 2 HIGHs (Rust UB via napi `&mut self` async aliasing; tokio runtime starvation). Closure REMOVED `flushPendingToTransport`; V2.3 retained `websocketConnect` only (static async, safe). Real-network round-trip via Node `ws` localhost relay validated end-to-end.
docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:606:   **V2.4 (cycle 4)** — sound `flushPendingToTransport` reintroduction via Arc<Mutex> refactor. Engine `c51df9f41f4` → `7123c6a57bb`. IDE `3ab02bbe732` → `8f0a44e19e9`. CollabSession wrapper refactored from `inner: CoreCollabSession` (&mut self methods) to `inner: Arc<parking_lot::Mutex<CoreCollabSession>>` (&self methods + internal lock + spawn_blocking for async). V2.3's 2 HIGHs STRUCTURALLY CLOSED (both auditors verified via source-walks of napi-derive-backend + lock_api + tokio). V2.4 audit found 2 NEW HIGHs: (1) V8-block UX hazard (sync method during pending flushPending blocks event loop — NOT a soundness hazard; deferred engine refactor V2.5+), (2) module docstring drift (Send+!Sync → Send+Sync). Both closed in V2.4 cycle.
docs/MASTER-PLAN.md:610:   **V2 backlog (next session V2.5+)**: engine refactor for V8-block hazard (clone-Arc-then-release OR Notify-based async), test-only blocking transport fixture, multi-window IDE demo, structured `Error.code` discrimination, `#[napi(strict)]` sweep, `#[must_use]` on `attach_transport<T>` (~80 sites), `willFlushSend()` helper, `LoopbackTransport.close()` binding, 3-way megaudit + V2 exit packet.

docs/phase5/5-7-v1-exit-packet.md:300:**Status update (2026-05-22, post-V2.4)**: V2 is 4-of-8 cycles done (V2.1, V2.2, V2.3, V2.4 SHIPPED). Remaining V2.5 (engine refactor for V8-block) → V2.6 (test fixture) → V2.7 (multi-window demo) → V2.8 (megaudit + V2 exit packet). Phase 5.8 still gated on V2 completing.
docs/phase5/v1-exit-packet.md:3:status: ACTIVE — Phase 5 V1 surface complete 2026-05-19. **D-1 SHIPPED 2026-05-20**. **5.3 SHIPPED 2026-05-20**. **5.5 V2 V3 V1 SHIPPED 2026-05-21**. **5.5 V2 V4 V1 SHIPPED 2026-05-21**. **5.7 V1 SHIPPED 2026-05-22**. **5.7 V2.1 + V2.2 + V2.3 + V2.4 SHIPPED 2026-05-22** (Transport binding 4-of-8 cycles done). See `docs/phase5/d-1-exit-packet.md` + `docs/phase5/5-3-exit-packet.md` + `docs/phase5/v2-v3-exit-packet.md` (V2 V3 V1) + `docs/phase5/v2-v4-v1-exit-packet.md` (V2 V4 V1) + `docs/phase5/5-7-v1-exit-packet.md` (5.7 V1 cross-repo). V2 exit packet pending (V2.8). Remaining: 5.7 V2.5 engine refactor for V8-block hazard (~1d), V2.6 test fixture (~half-day), V2.7 multi-window demo (~1-2d), V2.8 megaudit + V2 exit packet (~2-3d), then 5.7 V3 cell-grid UI (~1-2wk), 5.8 megaudit (~4-6d, after V2 complete).

docs/audits/2026-05-22-phase-5-7-v2-4-codex.md:12624:Closure: add a fake V2.3-shaped module with `Transport.websocketConnect` and all V2.2 methods, but no `CollabSession.prototype.flushPendingToTransport`, and assert the V2.4 missing-export message.
docs/audits/2026-05-22-phase-5-7-v2-4-codex.md:12626:OBS: same-session concurrent `flushPendingToTransport` calls are safe but can consume multiple Tokio blocking threads while losers wait on the mutex. This does not starve the worker pool; Tokio’s default blocking cap is 512, then tasks queue. For pathological duplicate calls or a permanently stuck transport, consider coalescing in-flight flushes or adding timeout/cancel semantics.
docs/audits/2026-05-22-phase-5-7-v2-4-codex.md:12651:Closure: add a test-only slow/blocking transport or delayed WebSocket fixture that holds `flushPendingToTransport` long enough to prove same-session calls serialize and sync calls block/release in order.

./docs/audits/2026-05-22-phase-5-7-v2-4-codex.md:12626:OBS: same-session concurrent `flushPendingToTransport` calls are safe but can consume multiple Tokio blocking threads while losers wait on the mutex. This does not starve the worker pool; Tokio’s default blocking cap is 512, then tasks queue. For pathological duplicate calls or a permanently stuck transport, consider coalescing in-flight flushes or adding timeout/cancel semantics.

     1	//! `ql-bindings-node` — VS Code extension binding (Phase 5.7 V1).
     2	//!
     3	//! **Phase 5.7 V1 (2026-05-22, this ship):** binds `CollabSession` for the
     4	//! quantlab VS Code fork's extension host. Replaces the Phase-6+ reserved
     5	//! placeholder this crate used to be.
     6	//!
     7	//! ## Architecture
     8	//!
     9	//! cdylib + napi-rs 3.x. The `#[napi]` derive macros generate the
    10	//! JS-facing class shape from the Rust struct. napi-rs handles:
    11	//!   - JS class lifecycle (`new`, finalize)
    12	//!   - `BigInt <-> u64` conversion for `PeerId`
    13	//!   - `Uint8Array <-> &[u8]`: `as_ref()` exposes a slice INTO the
    14	//!     JS-owned ArrayBuffer (verified via napi-rs 3.9.0
    15	//!     `arraybuffer.rs:653`'s `slice::from_raw_parts` on the SAB-or-AB
    16	//!     backing store). On export we hand ownership of a `Vec<u8>` to
    17	//!     JS via `Uint8Array::from`, which transfers the buffer rather
    18	//!     than copying.
    19	//!   - panic safety: **napi-rs 3.x does NOT wrap entry points in
    20	//!     `catch_unwind` by default** (Phase 5.7 V1 audit Codex H1 +
    21	//!     Opus H3, 2026-05-22 — verified by reading napi-derive-backend
    22	//!     5.0.4 `src/codegen/fn.rs:217-240`: the wrap is gated on
    23	//!     `#[napi(catch_unwind)]` opt-in). The V1 binding does NOT use
    24	//!     `catch_unwind` — instead, every `#[napi]` method
    25	//!     pre-validates inputs that could trigger engine-side `assert_*!`,
    26	//!     `panic!`, `unwrap`, or `expect`. Smoke caught one such hazard
    27	//!     (PeerId(0) → `CollabSession::new`'s `assert_ne!(peer, 0)` →
    28	//!     Node abort "failed to initiate panic, error 5"); the
    29	//!     `peer_id_from_bigint` helper now pre-rejects 0. V2+ each new
    30	//!     `#[napi]` method MUST sweep the engine for FFI-reachable
    31	//!     assertions and pre-validate the same way (see V2 backlog).
    32	//!
    33	//! ## V1 surface
    34	//!
    35	//! Single class `CollabSession` exposing the minimum methods that let
    36	//! TS code drive a round-trip:
    37	//!   - `new(peerId: bigint)`: wraps `ql_collab::CollabSession::new`
    38	//!   - static `fromSnapshot(peerId: bigint, bytes: Uint8Array)`:
    39	//!     wraps `from_snapshot`
    40	//!   - `appendPutValue(sheet, row, col, value)`: convenience for V1
    41	//!     (single Op variant; full Op enum binding deferred to V2)
    42	//!   - `exportBytes() -> Uint8Array`: snapshot
    43	//!   - `mergeBytes(bytes: Uint8Array) -> number`: returns merged op count
    44	//!   - `opCount() -> number`
    45	//!   - `pendingOpCount() -> number` (V2 V4 V1 step 2 helper)
    46	//!   - `hasPendingFlush() -> boolean` (V2 V3 step 3 helper)
    47	//!   - `peerId() -> bigint`
    48	//!
    49	//! ## V1 deferred (see `docs/phase5/5-7-v1-exit-packet.md`)
    50	//!
    51	//! Full V1-deferred-to-V2/V3 list lives in the V1 exit packet's
    52	//! "V1 deferred to V2" table. Summary: Transport binding
    53	//! (LoopbackTransport, WebSocketTransport, `attach_transport` /
    54	//! `detach_transport` / `has_transport` / `flush_to_transport` /
    55	//! `flush_delta_to_transport` / `flush_pending_to_transport` /
    56	//! `poll_remote` / `poll_remote_with_limit` /
    57	//! `transport_last_error` / `set_auto_flush_policy`), multi-window
    58	//! demo, undo/redo (UndoGroupGuard RAII translation), presence
    59	//! (PresenceState shape), full Op enum (beyond PutValue),
    60	//! Format/D-1 (FormatId enum), `rebuild_workbook` (D-3
    61	//! production-visible closure — Phase 5.7 V3), `discard_pending_ops`,
    62	//! `.qbook` persistence import/export, `#[napi(catch_unwind)]` opt-in
    63	//! for defense in depth, SharedArrayBuffer defensive copy, BigInt
    64	//! return for `opCount`/`pendingOpCount`/`mergeBytes`,
    65	//! `mergeBytesDelta` companion, `@napi-rs/cli` publish pipeline,
    66	//! Windows-specific `.dll` naming, CI `QUANTBOOK_REQUIRE_ENGINE=1`
    67	//! enforcement. V2 picks up Transport; V3 picks up cell-grid UI +
    68	//! persistence + `rebuild_workbook` wiring.
    69	//!
    70	//! ## Send + Sync
    71	//!
    72	//! **V2.4 (2026-05-22) UPDATE**: `CollabSession` is now `Send + Sync`
    73	//! (was `Send + !Sync` in V1+V2.1+V2.2+V2.3). The shape changed in V2.4
    74	//! from `inner: CoreCollabSession` to `inner: Arc<parking_lot::Mutex<CoreCollabSession>>`
    75	//! as the closure for V2.3 audit's HIGH-1 (Rust UB via napi `&mut self`
    76	//! async re-entry). The Arc<Mutex> composition adds Sync via interior
    77	//! mutability — multiple shared `&CollabSession` references are sound;
    78	//! mutation flows through the Mutex.
    79	//!
    80	//! **Compile-asserted proof** (lib.rs near end of file): both Send AND
    81	//! Sync are pinned via `assert_send + assert_sync` const-fn pattern.
    82	//! Per Rule 4: positive proof is required (this is the V2.4
    83	//! re-pinning of what was V1's `!Sync` claim).
    84	//!
    85	//! **Underlying composition** (Send + Sync verified by source-walks):
    86	//!   - `CoreCollabSession: Send + !Sync` (engine claim at
    87	//!     `ql-collab/src/session.rs:87`, unchanged).
    88	//!   - `parking_lot::Mutex<T>: Send + Sync` when `T: Send`
    89	//!     (verified per `lock_api-0.4.14/src/mutex.rs:144`'s
    90	//!     `impl<R: RawMutex + Sync, T: Send> Sync for Mutex<R, T>`).
    91	//!   - `Arc<T>: Send + Sync` when `T: Send + Sync`.
    92	//!   - Therefore the V2.4 binding wrapper composition is `Send + Sync`.
    93	//!
    94	//! **V2.4 V8-block hazard (Opus V2.4 HIGH-1, NOT runtime-unsound)**:
    95	//! all napi methods on `CollabSession` acquire `self.inner.lock()`.
    96	//! While `flushPendingToTransport`'s spawn_blocking task holds the
    97	//! mutex during a Condvar wait (potentially seconds), JS sync method
    98	//! calls on the SAME session block the V8 event loop waiting for the
    99	//! lock. The IDE UI freezes for the wait duration. This is a UX
   100	//! hazard, not a soundness hazard. V2.5+ work plan documented at the
   101	//! `flushPendingToTransport` method below. The TS-side caller
   102	//! discipline today is: don't call other session methods while
   103	//! `flushPendingToTransport` is awaiting.
   104	
   105	#![deny(clippy::all)]
   106	#![allow(clippy::missing_safety_doc)] // napi-rs generated wrappers
   107	
   108	use std::sync::Arc;
   109	
   110	use napi::bindgen_prelude::*;
   111	use napi_derive::napi;
   112	use parking_lot::Mutex;
   113	
   114	use ql_collab::AutoFlushPolicy as CoreAutoFlushPolicy;
   115	use ql_collab::CollabSession as CoreCollabSession;

   130	}
   131	
   132	/// **Phase 5.7 V1 megaudit closure (Codex HIGH, 2026-05-22):**
   133	/// Validate a JS Number argument that represents a u32-domain value.
   134	/// Rejects non-finite, negative, fractional, and out-of-u32 values
   135	/// with a distinct error message naming the calling method + parameter.
   136	///
   137	/// Why this exists: napi-rs's `napi_get_value_uint32` (used for `u32`
   138	/// params) applies ECMAScript `ToUint32`, silently coercing `-1` to
   139	/// `u32::MAX`, `NaN`/`Infinity` to `0`, fractions to floor-toward-zero.
   140	/// By taking the param as `f64` (raw `napi_get_value_double`) and
   141	/// validating here, we surface bad inputs as explicit JS Errors
   142	/// instead of corrupting workbook state.
   143	///
   144	/// **V2.2 audit closure (Codex LOW-1, 2026-05-22)**: added `method`
   145	/// context parameter so the error message names the actual calling
   146	/// method. Prior version hard-coded `"appendPutValue"` which produced
   147	/// confusing diagnostics when called from `pollRemoteWithLimit`.
   148	fn validate_u32_index(method: &str, name: &str, value: f64) -> Result<u32> {
   149	    if !value.is_finite() {
   150	        return Err(Error::from_reason(format!(
   151	            "{method}: {name} must be a finite non-negative integer, got {value}"
   152	        )));
   153	    }
   154	    if value < 0.0 {
   155	        return Err(Error::from_reason(format!(
   156	            "{method}: {name} must be a non-negative integer, got {value}"
   157	        )));
   158	    }
   159	    if value.fract() != 0.0 {
   160	        return Err(Error::from_reason(format!(
   161	            "{method}: {name} must be an integer, got {value}"
   162	        )));
   163	    }
   164	    if value > u32::MAX as f64 {
   165	        return Err(Error::from_reason(format!(
   166	            "{method}: {name} must be in [0, 4294967295] (u32::MAX), got {value}"
   167	        )));
   168	    }
   169	    Ok(value as u32)
   170	}

   897	    // ============================================================
   898	    //

   903	
   904	    #[cfg(feature = "test-fixtures")]
   905	    #[test]
   906	    fn blocking_transport_send_and_recv_are_noops() {
   907	        use super::BlockingTransport;
   908	        use std::sync::Condvar;
   909	        let release = std::sync::Arc::new((std::sync::Mutex::new(false), Condvar::new()));
   910	        let blocked = std::sync::Arc::new((std::sync::Mutex::new(false), Condvar::new()));
   911	        let mut t = BlockingTransport::new(0, release, blocked);
   912	        // send returns Ok, doesn't queue anything observable.
   913	        t.send(b"ignored").unwrap();
   914	        // try_recv returns None forever.
   915	        assert!(t.try_recv().unwrap().is_none());
   916	    }
   917	
   918	    #[cfg(feature = "test-fixtures")]
   919	    #[test]
   920	    fn blocking_transport_flush_pending_blocks_then_release_unblocks() {
   921	        use super::BlockingTransport;
   922	        use std::sync::Condvar;
   923	        use std::time::Instant;
   924	        let release = std::sync::Arc::new((std::sync::Mutex::new(false), Condvar::new()));
   925	        let blocked = std::sync::Arc::new((std::sync::Mutex::new(false), Condvar::new()));
   926	        let mut t = BlockingTransport::new(
   927	            5000,
   928	            std::sync::Arc::clone(&release),
   929	            std::sync::Arc::clone(&blocked),
   930	        );
   931	
   932	        // Spawn a thread that releases after 50ms. The wait should
   933	        // complete well before the 5000ms upper bound.
   934	        let release_clone = std::sync::Arc::clone(&release);
   935	        std::thread::spawn(move || {
   936	            std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(50));
   937	            *release_clone.0.lock().unwrap() = true;
   938	            release_clone.1.notify_all();
   939	        });
   940	
   941	        let start = Instant::now();
   942	        t.flush_pending().expect("flush_pending unblocked");
   943	        let elapsed = start.elapsed();
   944	        // Should complete within ~250ms (50ms target + scheduler
   945	        // jitter). Way below the 5000ms upper bound.
   946	        assert!(
   947	            elapsed < std::time::Duration::from_millis(500),
   948	            "flush_pending should unblock promptly after release; took {elapsed:?}"
   949	        );
   950	    }
   951	
   952	    #[cfg(feature = "test-fixtures")]
   953	    #[test]
   954	    fn blocking_transport_flush_pending_signals_blocked_flag() {
   955	        // Pins the V2.6 deterministic-signal contract: the
   956	        // `blocked` Condvar fires BEFORE the wait on `release`
   957	        // starts. JS `waitUntilBlocked()` relies on this.
   958	        use super::BlockingTransport;
   959	        use std::sync::Condvar;
   960	        let release = std::sync::Arc::new((std::sync::Mutex::new(false), Condvar::new()));
   961	        let blocked = std::sync::Arc::new((std::sync::Mutex::new(false), Condvar::new()));
   962	        let mut t = BlockingTransport::new(
   963	            1000,
   964	            std::sync::Arc::clone(&release),
   965	            std::sync::Arc::clone(&blocked),
   966	        );
   967	
   968	        // Spawn the flush in a thread.
   969	        let handle = std::thread::spawn(move || t.flush_pending());
   970	
   971	        // Wait for `blocked` flag.
   972	        {
   973	            let (lock, cv) = &*blocked;
   974	            let mut flag = lock.lock().unwrap();
   975	            while !*flag {
   976	                let (new_flag, timeout) = cv
   977	                    .wait_timeout(flag, std::time::Duration::from_secs(2))
   978	                    .unwrap();
   979	                flag = new_flag;
   980	                if timeout.timed_out() {
   981	                    panic!("blocked flag never set within 2s");
   982	                }
   983	            }
   984	        }
   985	
   986	        // Release the flush.
   987	        *release.0.lock().unwrap() = true;
   988	        release.1.notify_all();
   989	
   990	        // Flush thread completes.
   991	        handle.join().unwrap().expect("flush_pending ok");
   992	    }

   994	    #[cfg(feature = "test-fixtures")]
   995	    #[test]

Phase 5.7 V2.5 + V2.6 engine — Transport::ack_handle trait + BlockingTransport fixture (closes Opus V2.4 HIGH-1 V8-block UX hazard)

V2.5 closes Opus V2.4 HIGH-1 (V8-block UX hazard) by Option A from
the V2.4 closure plan: split the engine's flush_pending_to_transport
into "extract ack handle under outer lock" + "wait on detached
handle without outer lock". Codex-verified PASS-WITH-FINDINGS at
plan time (transcript: .codex-v2-5-plan-review.out); all 4 MEDIUM +
1 LOW findings applied to design before implementation.

Pair commit (IDE): [closure commit hash].

## What ships

### `Transport` trait extension (additive, non-breaking)

`crates/ql-collab/src/transport.rs`:
- **`FlushAck` trait** — detached drain-wait handle. `Send`, takes
  `&self`, `wait_for_drain() -> Result<(), TransportError>`.
- **`Transport::ack_handle(&self) -> Option<Box<dyn FlushAck + Send>>`**
  default-`None` method. Default impl preserves existing semantics
  for Loopback / Noop (no async-flush). Production impls override
  (WebSocketTransport below). Codex M4: NO `Send` supertrait added
  to `Transport` — adding one is breaking even for engine-internal
  callers. The trait's existing call sites that need Send already
  bound `Box<dyn Transport + Send>` explicitly.

### `WebSocketProgressAckHandle` (production impl)

`crates/ql-collab-ws/src/lib.rs`:
- New `WebSocketProgressAckHandle { target: u64, progress: Arc<...>, closed: Arc<...> }`.
- `impl Transport::ack_handle for WebSocketTransport` captures
  `queued_count.load(SeqCst)` as `target` AT THIS CALL (Codex M1
  contract: sends queued AFTER ack_handle() returns do NOT extend
  the wait — preserves the documented "previously queued" semantic).
- `impl FlushAck for WebSocketProgressAckHandle::wait_for_drain`
  mirrors `WebSocketTransport::flush_pending`'s loop on a CAPTURED
  target. Same Closed-on-entry + closed-mid-wait + poisoned-mutex
  contract.
- 3 new integration tests in `tests/websocket_transport.rs`:
  - `ack_handle_wait_for_drain_matches_flush_pending`
  - `ack_handle_target_captured_at_call_not_at_wait`
  - `ack_handle_survives_transport_drop_returns_closed`

### `BlockingTransport` (V2.6 test fixture, feature-gated)

`crates/ql-collab/src/transport.rs`:
- New `BlockingTransport` + `BlockingAckHandle` behind
  `#[cfg(feature = "test-fixtures")]` (Codex Q4 fix).
- `flush_pending` blocks until externally-released Condvar OR
  `block_ms` upper bound elapses. Defensive timeout prevents hangs.
- Two Arc<(Mutex<bool>, Condvar)> fields: `release` (caller
  signals) + `blocked` (transport signals "wait entered" — Codex M3
  determinism point).
- 5 new Rust unit tests verify the fixture in isolation.

### `CollabSession::flush_pending_handle` proxy

`crates/ql-collab/src/session.rs`:
- New `pub fn flush_pending_handle(&self) -> Option<Box<dyn FlushAck + Send>>`.
- Takes `&self` (no mutation; proxies via `transport.as_ref()?.ack_handle()`).
- Enables the napi binding's V2.5 lock-release pattern.

### Re-exports (Codex L1 fix)

`crates/ql-collab/src/lib.rs`:
- Re-export `FlushAck` alongside existing `Transport` exports.
- Re-export `BlockingTransport` + `BlockingAckHandle` behind the
  `test-fixtures` feature.

### Feature flag (Codex Q4 fix)

`crates/ql-collab/Cargo.toml`:
- New `test-fixtures = []` feature gates `BlockingTransport` +
  `BlockingAckHandle`. Production builds without the feature do NOT
  carry the fixture; mocha cdylib enables it unconditionally.

`crates/ql-bindings-node/Cargo.toml`:
- `ql-collab` dep declared with `features = ["test-fixtures"]` so
  the cdylib build flow stays unchanged (no `--features` flag
  needed at build time). V2 backlog: "harden test-fixture exposure
  for production cdylib builds" (post-V2 product hardening).

### napi binding refactor — THE V2.5 V8-BLOCK CLOSURE

`crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs`:

`flushPendingToTransport` REWRITTEN from V2.4 pattern:

```rust
// V2.4 pattern (THE BUG — V8-block):
let inner = Arc::clone(&self.inner);
tokio::task::spawn_blocking(move || {
    let mut guard = inner.lock();           // <-- HELD across Condvar wait
    guard.flush_pending_to_transport()      // <-- Condvar wait happens here
}).await...

// V2.5 pattern (V8-block CLOSED):
let handle_opt: Option<Box<dyn ql_collab::FlushAck + Send>> = {
    let inner = self.inner.lock();
    inner.flush_pending_handle()
};                                          // <-- session lock RELEASED here
let Some(handle) = handle_opt else { return Ok(()) };
tokio::task::spawn_blocking(move || handle.wait_for_drain())
    .await...
```

Concurrent JS sync methods can now acquire `self.inner.lock()`
immediately while the Condvar wait runs in the blocking-pool
thread on cloned Arcs. The V8 event loop stays responsive.

### `BlockingTransportFixture` napi class (V2.6 test fixture)

`crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs`:
- New `#[napi] pub struct BlockingTransportFixture` wrapping an
  `Option<ql_collab::BlockingTransport>` + the shared release +
  blocked Arc<(Mutex, Condvar)>.
- Methods: `new(blockMs: number)` constructor, `takeTransport()`,
  `release()`, `waitUntilBlocked()`.
- `waitUntilBlocked()` is async (uses spawn_blocking internally)
  so JS mocha tests have a deterministic synchronization point —
  closes Codex M3 (without this signal, V2.5 contract tests could
  race and pass vacuously).
- Mirrors V2.1 `LoopbackPair`'s single-use take pattern (Codex M2
  fix — separately-constructed `BlockingTransport` is NOT
  attachable via the existing napi attachTransport surface).

### Rule 4 compile-asserts

- `WebSocketProgressAckHandle: Send` (lib.rs `_ASSERT_WEBSOCKET_PROGRESS_ACK_HANDLE_SEND`).
- `BlockingTransport: Send` + `BlockingAckHandle: Send`
  (transport.rs `_ASSERT_BLOCKING_TRANSPORT_SEND`).
- `BlockingTransportFixture: Send` (binding lib.rs
  `_ASSERT_BINDING_BLOCKING_TRANSPORT_FIXTURE_SEND`).

`.codex-v2-5-plan-review.out`. 4 MEDIUM + 1 LOW findings all

- M1 — target captured in `ack_handle()`, not `wait_for_drain`. ✓

- Q4 — feature-gate BlockingTransport (Codex preferred over production
  cdylib; we kept feature gate explicit while enabling it

**Risks 5-9 from Codex review** — all LOW, no changes needed.

## What ships

### `src/quantbook/types.ts`

- New `BlockingTransportFixtureInstance` + `BlockingTransportFixtureConstructor`
  interfaces. Mirror V2.1 LoopbackPair single-use take pattern:
  `takeTransport(): TransportInstance`, `release(): void`,
  `waitUntilBlocked(): Promise<void>`.
- Added `readonly BlockingTransportFixture: BlockingTransportFixtureConstructor`
  to `QuantbookNativeModule`.
- Rewrote `flushPendingToTransport` JSDoc to remove V2.4 V8-block
  warning + add V2.5 closure note ("session lock is released
  before the Condvar wait; concurrent sync methods on the same
  session do NOT block the V8 event loop").

### `src/quantbook/loader.ts`

- Added shape-check for `BlockingTransportFixture` export. A binary
  built without `test-fixtures` (e.g., a future production-build
  flow that strips it) would fail late with `engine.BlockingTransportFixture
  is not a constructor` when V2.5 contract tests instantiate it.
  Catch at the loader boundary instead.

### `test/quantbook-roundtrip.test.ts` — V2.5 contract tests

**Replaced** the 2 V2.4 vacuous Loopback-based "smoke tests"
(Opus V2.4 MEDIUM-2: vacuous on Loopback) with V2.5 contract tests
using `BlockingTransportFixture`:

1. **V2.5 contract: flushPendingToTransport does NOT block sync
   methods on the same session (BlockingTransportFixture)** — THE
   V2.5 PIN. Closes Opus V2.4 HIGH-1. Pattern:
   - `BlockingTransportFixture(1000)` → blocks 1000ms
   - attach + `flushPendingToTransport()` (returns Promise)
   - `await fixture.waitUntilBlocked()` (Codex M3 determinism)
   - measure `session.opCount()` elapsed
   - assert `< blockMs/2 = 500ms`
   - Under V2.4 binding pattern this would block ~1000ms (because
     the spawn_blocking task held the session lock during the
     Condvar wait). Under V2.5: returns immediately.

2. **V2.5 contract: concurrent flushPendingToTransport calls
   serialize through ack handle (BlockingTransportFixture)** —
   verifies two parallel flushPending promises both resolve after
   a single `release()`.

3. **V2.6 BlockingTransportFixture: takeTransport is single-use** —
   mirror of V2.1 LoopbackPair pattern.

4. **V2.6 BlockingTransportFixture: blockMs upper bound prevents
   indefinite hang** — defensive timeout test.

5. **V2.6 BlockingTransportFixture: release() is idempotent**.

6. **V2.6 BlockingTransportFixture: constructor rejects non-finite
   blockMs** — validates the `validate_u32_index` ToUint32-hygiene
   pattern (NaN, negative, fractional all rejected).

7. **stale V2.4-shaped binary (missing V2.6 BlockingTransportFixture)
   is rejected at the boundary** — V2.6 loader-skew detection
   test. A binary built without the `test-fixtures` feature on
   ql-collab lacks `BlockingTransportFixture` export; the loader
   now catches this.

The V2.4 "stale V2.3-shaped binary" test is RETAINED (V2.4 closure
Codex LOW-3 pattern still validates the V2.4 prototype check) +
updated to include a fake `BlockingTransportFixture` export so it
exercises ONLY the V2.4 flushPendingToTransport check, not the V2.6
one.

# Codex plan review: Phase 5.7 V2.5 + V2.6 — engine refactor for V8-block UX hazard

## Verdict format

Report at top:
- **VERDICT**: PASS / PASS-WITH-FINDINGS / FAIL
- One-sentence summary of the strongest concern.

Then a sections-based response with each finding rated H/M/L and **with a file:line citation** for each technical claim.

## Context

**Repo**: quantlab-quantbook engine + its IDE binding. State at HEAD:
- engine `9eece27cf77` on branch `feat/quantbook-engine`
- IDE  `8f0a44e19e9` on branch `feat/visualise-v1`

**Engine root** (for source walks): `/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/`. The IDE lives at `/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab/extensions/quantlab/`.

codex
**VERDICT**: PASS-WITH-FINDINGS

**Strongest concern**: the V8-block hazard is closed, but the production-loaded `ql-bindings-node` cdylib now unconditionally exports a test fixture that can create indefinite blocking waits if misused.

**Findings**

- **M1: Production cdylib carries a deliberate DoS footgun.** `ql-bindings-node` enables `ql-collab/test-fixtures` unconditionally, so `BlockingTransportFixture` is present in the IDE-loaded binary ([Cargo.toml](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/Cargo.toml:51)). Its constructor documents `blockMs = 0` as indefinite ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:1138)), and the engine wait loop really waits forever until release when `block_ms == 0` ([transport.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab/src/transport.rs:664)). A malicious or buggy in-process IDE caller can attach it and park `flushPendingToTransport` blocking-pool tasks. Not memory unsafe, but not “harmless” in an adversarial availability model.

- **L1: Top-level binding docs still describe the V2.4 V8-block hazard as current.** The module doc says `flushPendingToTransport` holds the mutex during the Condvar wait and tells TS callers not to call other session methods while awaiting it ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:94)). That contradicts the actual V2.5 method body, which drops the lock before `spawn_blocking` ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:829)).

- **L2: Two tests overclaim the behavior they prove.** `ack_handle_survives_transport_drop_returns_closed` starts the wait after `drop(ws)` and accepts either `Ok(())` or `Err(Closed)`, so it does not strictly prove “mid-wait detach returns Closed” ([websocket_transport.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab-ws/tests/websocket_transport.rs:1196)). The mocha “serialize through ack handle” test actually creates two independent waits and only verifies both resolve after one release ([quantbook-roundtrip.test.ts](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab/extensions/quantlab/test/quantbook-roundtrip.test.ts:1263)).

- **L3: Stale-binary UX could be more actionable.** The loader detects missing `BlockingTransportFixture` ([loader.ts](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab/extensions/quantlab/src/quantbook/loader.ts:295)), but the error only says rebuild `ql-bindings-node` ([loader.ts](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab/extensions/quantlab/src/quantbook/loader.ts:303)). If the missing export is specifically a stripped fixture build, mention `ql-collab`’s `test-fixtures` feature.

**V8-Block Closure Verdict**

Verified closed. `flushPendingToTransport` extracts `inner.flush_pending_handle()` inside a scoped lock ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:829)), the guard drops at the closing brace, and the blocking task only moves `handle` and calls `handle.wait_for_drain()` ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:843)). No `self.inner` access occurs inside the blocking wait.

**Q1-Q9 Risk Answers**

1. **detachTransport mid-wait**: implementation verified. `detachTransport` drops the boxed transport ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:510)); `WebSocketTransport::drop` sets `closed = true` and notifies waiters ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab-ws/src/lib.rs:633)); handle wait observes `closed` ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab-ws/src/lib.rs:815)). Test coverage is weaker than the claim.
2. **CI flake threshold**: 500ms is realistic for an immediate `opCount()` lock acquisition, and still catches the old ~1000ms failure. If CI is noisy, use `blockMs=2000` and threshold `<1000` rather than weakening against a 1000ms block.
3. **Concurrent flush soundness**: verified sound; not serialized. Each call gets its own `FlushAck` handle. Rename the test or add timing assertions if serialization is intended.
4. **`blockMs == 0`**: semantics verified, but not pinned by a dedicated test. Misuse can hang until Mocha timeout.
5. **Fixture Send claim**: verified. Engine asserts `BlockingTransport`/`BlockingAckHandle: Send` ([transport.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab/src/transport.rs:743)); binding asserts `BlockingTransportFixture: Send` ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:1303)).
6. **Stale-binary UX**: verified detection; message should mention the feature flag for this specific missing export.
7. **Production fixture**: not memory unsafe, but not harmless. Availability DoS is possible from in-process caller code.
8. **TS type-safety**: usable without casts; consumed fixture calls fail at runtime via `takeTransport already called` ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:1175)).
9. **Trait stability**: verified additive. `Transport` has no `Send` supertrait and `ack_handle` is default-`None` ([transport.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab/src/transport.rs:301)); all existing `impl Transport for` sites remain valid.

**Recommended Before Closure**

Update the stale top-level binding doc, rename/tighten the two overclaiming tests, and improve the loader error. For backlog: gate or strip `BlockingTransportFixture` from production artifacts, then prioritize V2.7 multi-window demo, structured `Error.code`, and the V2.8 megaudit/exit packet.

Its constructor documents `blockMs = 0` as indefinite ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:1138)), and the engine wait loop really waits forever until release when `block_ms == 0` ([transport.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab/src/transport.rs:664)). A malicious or buggy in-process IDE caller can attach it and park `flushPendingToTransport` blocking-pool tasks. Not memory unsafe, but not “harmless” in an adversarial availability model.

- **L1: Top-level binding docs still describe the V2.4 V8-block hazard as current.** The module doc says `flushPendingToTransport` holds the mutex during the Condvar wait and tells TS callers not to call other session methods while awaiting it ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:94)). That contradicts the actual V2.5 method body, which drops the lock before `spawn_blocking` ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:829)).

- **L2: Two tests overclaim the behavior they prove.** `ack_handle_survives_transport_drop_returns_closed` starts the wait after `drop(ws)` and accepts either `Ok(())` or `Err(Closed)`, so it does not strictly prove “mid-wait detach returns Closed” ([websocket_transport.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab-ws/tests/websocket_transport.rs:1196)). The mocha “serialize through ack handle” test actually creates two independent waits and only verifies both resolve after one release ([quantbook-roundtrip.test.ts](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab/extensions/quantlab/test/quantbook-roundtrip.test.ts:1263)).

- **L3: Stale-binary UX could be more actionable.** The loader detects missing `BlockingTransportFixture` ([loader.ts](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab/extensions/quantlab/src/quantbook/loader.ts:295)), but the error only says rebuild `ql-bindings-node` ([loader.ts](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab/extensions/quantlab/src/quantbook/loader.ts:303)). If the missing export is specifically a stripped fixture build, mention `ql-collab`’s `test-fixtures` feature.

**V8-Block Closure Verdict**

Verified closed. `flushPendingToTransport` extracts `inner.flush_pending_handle()` inside a scoped lock ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:829)), the guard drops at the closing brace, and the blocking task only moves `handle` and calls `handle.wait_for_drain()` ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:843)). No `self.inner` access occurs inside the blocking wait.

**Q1-Q9 Risk Answers**

1. **detachTransport mid-wait**: implementation verified. `detachTransport` drops the boxed transport ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:510)); `WebSocketTransport::drop` sets `closed = true` and notifies waiters ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab-ws/src/lib.rs:633)); handle wait observes `closed` ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab-ws/src/lib.rs:815)). Test coverage is weaker than the claim.
2. **CI flake threshold**: 500ms is realistic for an immediate `opCount()` lock acquisition, and still catches the old ~1000ms failure. If CI is noisy, use `blockMs=2000` and threshold `<1000` rather than weakening against a 1000ms block.
3. **Concurrent flush soundness**: verified sound; not serialized. Each call gets its own `FlushAck` handle. Rename the test or add timing assertions if serialization is intended.
4. **`blockMs == 0`**: semantics verified, but not pinned by a dedicated test. Misuse can hang until Mocha timeout.
5. **Fixture Send claim**: verified. Engine asserts `BlockingTransport`/`BlockingAckHandle: Send` ([transport.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab/src/transport.rs:743)); binding asserts `BlockingTransportFixture: Send` ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:1303)).
6. **Stale-binary UX**: verified detection; message should mention the feature flag for this specific missing export.
7. **Production fixture**: not memory unsafe, but not harmless. Availability DoS is possible from in-process caller code.
8. **TS type-safety**: usable without casts; consumed fixture calls fail at runtime via `takeTransport already called` ([lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:1175)).
9. **Trait stability**: verified additive. `Transport` has no `Send` supertrait and `ack_handle` is default-`None` ([transport.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-collab/src/transport.rs:301)); all existing `impl Transport for` sites remain valid.

**Recommended Before Closure**

Update the stale top-level binding doc, rename/tighten the two overclaiming tests, and improve the loader error. For backlog: gate or strip `BlockingTransportFixture` from production artifacts, then prioritize V2.7 multi-window demo, structured `Error.code`, and the V2.8 megaudit/exit packet.
